# III

# The Tattvaprakāśa of Bhoja Paramāra

॥ ॐ शिवाभ्यां नमः ॥

तत्त्वप्रकाशः

(श्रीकुमाराघोरशिवाचार्ययोः तात्पर्यदीपिकावृत्तिभ्यां सहितः)

विद्वचन एको व्यापी नित्यः सततोदितः प्रभुश्शान्तः।

जयति जगदेकबीजं सर्वानुग्राहकश्शंभुः॥१॥

नोदयति यत्र नश्यति निर्वाति न निर्वृत्तिं प्रयच्छति च।

ज्ञानक्रियास्वभावं तत्तेजः शाम्भवं जयति॥२॥

शक्तो यया स शम्भुर्मुक्तौ मुक्तौ च पशुगणस्यास्य।

तामेकां चिद्रूपाभाद्यां सर्वात्मनाऽस्मि नतः॥३॥

लोकानुग्रहहेतोः करुणारसन्निर्भरेण चित्तेन।

तत्त्वप्रकाश एषोऽस्माभिः क्रियते समासेन॥४॥

शैवागमेषु मुख्यं पतिपशुपाशा इति क्रमात् त्रितयम्।

तत्र पतिः शिव उक्तः पशवो ह्यणवोऽर्थपञ्चकं पाशः॥५॥

मुक्तात्मानोऽपि शिवाः किन्त्वेते तत्प्रसादतो मुक्ताः।

सोऽनादिमुक्त एको विज्ञेयः पञ्चमन्त्रतनुः॥६॥

पञ्चविधं तत्कृत्य सृष्टिस्थितिसंहृतितिरोभावाः।

तद्वदनुग्रहकरणं प्रोक्तं सततोदितस्यास्य॥७॥

पशवस्त्रिविधा ज्ञेयाः विज्ञानप्रलयकेवली सकलः।

मलंयुक्तस्तत्राद्यो मलकर्मयुतो द्वितीयः स्यात्॥८॥

THE TATTVAPRAKĀŚA OF BHOJA PARAMĀRA | 113

<!-- page 113 src=20260817_163158.jpg -->

मलामायाकर्मयुतः सकलस्तेषु द्विधा भवेदाद्यः ।
आद्यः समाप्तकलुषोऽसमाप्तकलुषो द्वितीयः स्यात् ॥६॥

आध्याननुगृह्य शिवो विद्येशत्वे नियोजयत्यष्टौ ।
मन्त्राश्च करोत्यपरान् ते चोक्ताः कोटयः सप्त ॥१०॥

प्रलयाकलेषु येषां पको मलकर्मणी व्रजन्त्यन्ये ।
पुर्यष्टकदेहयुताः योनिषु निखिलासु कर्मवशात् ॥११॥

स्यात् पुर्यष्टकमन्तःकरणं धीकर्मकरणानि ।
काश्चिदनुगृह्य वितरति भुवनपतित्वं महेश्वरस्तेषाम् ॥१२॥

शेषा भवन्ति सकलाः कलादियोगादहर्मुखे काले ।
शतमष्टादश तेषां कुरुते स्वयमेव मन्त्रेशान् ॥१३॥

तत्राष्टौ मण्डलिनः क्रोधाद्यास्तत्समाश्च वीरेशः ।
श्रीकण्ठः शतरूद्राः शतमित्यष्टादशाभ्यधिकम् ॥१४॥

परिपाकमलानेतानुत्सादनहेतुशक्तिपातेन ।
योजयति परे तत्त्वे स दीक्षयाऽऽवार्यमूर्तिस्थः ॥१५॥

बद्धान् शेषानपरान् विनियुङ्क्ते भोगभुक्तये पुंसः ।
तत्कर्मणामनुगमातदित्येवं कीर्तिताः पशवः ॥१६॥

पाशाश्चतुर्विधाः स्युः पुंसो मलकर्मजौ मतौ प्रथमौ ।
मायीयतिरोधायकशिवशक्तिसमुद्भवौ चान्यौ ॥१७॥

एको ह्यनेकशक्तिर्दृक्क्रिययोश्छादको मलः पुंसः ।
तुषतण्डुल वज्ज्ञेयस्ताम्राश्रितकालिमावद् वा ॥१८॥

कर्मानादिप्रोक्तं धर्माधर्मात्मकं विचित्रं च ।
माया च वस्तुरूपा मूलं विश्वस्य नित्या सा ॥१९॥

पाशानुग्राहित्वात् पुरुषतिरोधायिका विभोश्शक्तिः ।
पाशत्वेनाभिहिता पाशाश्च चतुर्विधास्त्वेवम् ॥२०॥

शुद्धानि पञ्चतत्वान्याद्यन्तेषु स्मरन्ति शिवतत्त्वम् ।
शक्तिसदाशिवतत्त्वे ईश्वरविद्याख्यतत्त्वे च ॥२१॥

पुंसोऽज्ञकर्तुतार्थं मायातस्तत्त्वपञ्चकं भवति ।
कालो नियतिश्च तथा कला च विद्या च रागश्च ॥२२॥

अव्यक्तं मायातो गुणतत्त्वं तदनु बुद्ध्यहङ्कारौ ।
चेतो धीकर्मेन्द्रियतन्मात्राण्यनु च पञ्चभूतानि ॥२३॥

114

THE UDAYĒŠVARA TEMPLE

<!-- page 114 src=20260817_163203.jpg -->

पुरुषस्य भोगहेतोः प्रधानतो विशतिर्भवेत् त्र्यधिका।
ग्रन्थात् प्रकृतिगुणानां नात्यन्तं वस्तुतो भेदः ॥२४॥

व्यापकमेकं नित्यं कारणमखिलस्य तत्त्वजातस्य।
ज्ञानक्रियास्वभावं शिवतत्त्वं जगदुराचार्याः ॥२५॥

अभिजिलीय निखिला इच्छाद्याः शक्तयः स्वकं कार्यम्।
कुर्वन्ति तेन तदिदं सर्वानुग्राहकं प्राहुः ॥२६॥

विदविदनुग्रहहेतोरस्य सिसृक्षोर्ध्व आद्य उन्मेषः।
तच्छक्तितत्त्वमभिहितमविभागापन्नमस्यैव ॥२७॥

ज्ञानक्रियाख्यशक्त्योरपकर्षोत्कर्षयोरभावेन।
दः प्रसस्तं प्राहुः सदाशिवाख्यं बुधास्तत्त्वम् ॥२८॥

न्वप्नयति यत्र शक्तिरज्ञानाख्योद्भक्तितां क्रिया भजते।
ईश्वरतत्त्वं तदिह प्रोक्तं सर्वार्थकर्तुं सदा ॥२९॥

न्वप्नयति कर्तृशक्तिरज्ञानाख्योद्भक्तमश्नुते यत्र।
तत्त्वं विद्याख्यं प्रकाशकं ज्ञानरूपत्वात् ॥३०॥

नादो बिन्दुः सकलौ सादाख्यन्तत्वमाश्रितौ कथितौ।
विदेशाः पुनरैशे मन्त्रा विद्याश्च विद्याख्ये ॥३१॥

पञ्चानांनप्येषां न हि क्रमोऽस्तीह कालरहितत्वात्।
व्यापारदशादेषां विहिता खलु कल्पना शास्त्रे ॥३२॥

तत्त्वं वस्तुत एकं शिवसंज्ञं चित्रशक्तिशतखचितम्।
शक्तिव्यापुतिभेदात् तस्यैते कल्पिता भेदाः ॥३३॥

विदविदनुग्रहहेतोः रूपाण्येतानि स प्रभुः कृत्वा।
कुरुते वितामनुग्रहमनादिमलरुद्धशक्तीनाम् ॥३४॥

मुक्तिं मुक्तिमणूनां स्वव्यापारे समर्थताधानम्।
जडदर्गस्य विधत्ते सर्वानुग्राहकः शभु ॥३५॥

विदनुग्रहस्त्वयं किल यन्मोक्षशिवसमानतारूपः।
सोऽनादित्वात्कर्मण इह भोगमृते न याति संसिद्धिम् ॥३६॥

तेन विमुस्तदुमुक्त्यै कुरुते तनुकरणभुवननिष्पत्तिम्।
कर्त्रां विना न कार्यं न तथोपादानकरणाभ्याम् ॥३७॥

शक्त्य इहास्य करणं मायोपादानमिष्यते सूक्ष्मा।
एका नित्या व्यापिन्यनादिनिधना शिवासक्ताः ॥३८॥

THE TATTVAPRAKĀSA OF BHOJA PARAMĀRA | 115

<!-- page 115 src=20260817_163214.jpg -->

साधारणी नराणां कारणमपि चेयमखिलमुवनानाम् ।
निखिलजनकर्मखचिता स्वभावतो मोहसङ्गननी ॥३६॥

कर्मण्यवेक्ष्य शम्भुर्मायां विक्षोभ्य शक्तिभिः स्वाभिः ।
प्रतिपुरुषं भोगार्थं वपुषि करणानि चाघते ॥४०॥

नानाविधशक्तिमयी सा जनयति कालतत्त्वमेवादौ ।
भाविभवद्भूतमयं कलयति जगदेष कालोऽतः ॥४१॥

नियतिर्नियमनरूपा मायातः साऽयनन्तरं भवति ।
नियमयति येन निखिलं तेनेयं नियतिरित्युक्ता ॥४२॥

मायातस्तदनु कला मलं नृणामेकतस्तु कलयित्वा ।
व्यञ्जयति कर्तृशक्ति कलेति तेनेह कथितेयम् ॥४३॥

कालेन नियत्या चाप्युपसर्जनतामुपैतया सततम् ।
विदधाति व्यापारन्निजमेषा ह्यवनिपर्यन्तम् ॥४४॥

उद्बुद्धकर्तृशक्तेः पुंसो विषयप्रदर्शननिमित्तम् ।
विद्यातत्त्वं सूते प्रकाशरूपं कलैवैषा ॥४५॥

आवरणं भित्त्वैषा ज्ञानाख्यायाः स्वकर्मणा शक्तेः ।
दर्शयति विषयजातं करणं सेहात्मनः परमम् ॥४६॥

बुद्धिर्यदास्य भोग्या सुखादिरूपा तदा भवेत्करणम् ।
विद्यैवं करणं स्याद् विषयग्रहणे पुनर्बुद्धिः ॥४७॥

रागोऽभिष्वङ्गात्मा विषयच्छेदं विनैव सामान्यः ।
पुरुषप्रवृत्तिहेतुर्विलक्षणो बुद्धिधर्मेभ्यः ॥४८॥

तत्त्वैरेभिः कलितो भोक्तृत्वदशां यदा पशुनीतः ।
पुरुषाख्यतां तदाऽयं लभते तत्त्वेषु गणनां च ॥४९॥

पुरुषस्य हि भोगार्थं कलैव तस्मात् प्रसूयतेऽव्यक्तम् ।
अनभिव्यक्तगुणत्वादव्यपदेश्यं तदव्यक्तम् ॥५०॥

अव्यक्ताद् गुणतत्त्वं प्रख्याव्यापारनियमनरूपमिह ।
सत्त्वं रजस्तमोऽपि च सुखदुःखे मोह इत्यपि च ॥५१॥

भवति गुणत्रयतो धीर्विषयाध्यवसायरूपिणी चेह ।
सापि त्रिविधा गुणतः प्रोक्ता कर्मानुसारेण ॥५२॥

स्यात् त्रिविधोऽहङ्काकारो जीवनसंरम्भगर्दरूपोऽयम् ।
सभेदादस्य सतो विषयो व्यवहार्यतामेति ॥५३॥

116 | THE UDAYESVARA TEMPLE

<!-- page 116 src=20260817_163217.jpg -->

साधिकराजसतामसभेदेन स जायते पुनरत्रेधा ।
स च वैकारिकतैजसभूतादिकनामभिः समुच्छ्रसिति ॥५४॥

तैजसतस्तत्र मनो वैकारिकतो भवन्ति चाक्षाणि ।
भूतादेस्तन्मात्राण्येषां सर्गोऽयमेतस्मात् ॥५५॥

इच्छारूपं हि मनो व्यापारस्तस्य भवति सङ्कल्पः ।
बुध्यक्षाणि श्रोत्रं त्वग्दृग्जिह्वा च नासा च ॥५६॥

ग्राह्यास्तेषां शब्दः स्पर्शो रूपं रसश्च गन्धश्च ।
इत्येतेषां विषयाः क्रमेण पञ्चापि पञ्चानाम् ॥५७॥

शब्दादीनां ग्रहणं व्यापाराः कीर्तिताः क्रमेणैषाम् ।
वाक्पाणिपादपायूपस्थाः कर्मेन्द्रियाणि स्युः ॥५८॥

वचनादानविहरणोत्सर्गानन्दाश्च कर्मैषाम् ।
अन्तःकरणं त्रेधा बुद्धिरहंकारचेतसी चेति ॥५९॥

कर्मेन्द्रियबुद्धीन्द्रियभेदाद् बाह्यं पुनर्भवेदशधा ।
तन्मात्रेभ्यः खपवनतेजोऽम्भःक्ष्मेति पञ्चभूतानि ॥६०॥

तन्मात्राण्यविशेषाः शब्दादीनां प्रकीर्तिताः पञ्च ।
भूतानि पञ्च तेभ्यो भवन्ति तान्येकगुणवृद्ध्या ॥६१॥

अवकाशव्यूहनपाकसंग्रहाधारणं क्रमादेषाम् ।
व्यापारा भूतानां व्योमादीनां समुद्दिष्टाः ॥६२॥

कार्यमिदं दशधा यत् तत्करणैः कार्यते समाविश्य ।
करणान्यसमर्थत्वात्कार्यं संश्रित्य चेष्टन्ते ॥६३॥

पञ्चानामाद्यानां चिद्रूपतयान्वयः परेषां तु ।
मायाद्यानां द्वेधा सप्तानां कीर्तितः शैवे ॥६४॥

अव्यक्तप्रभृतीनां गुणैश्च सुखदुःखमोहरूपतया ।
अन्वय इह निखिलानां दशके ह्यन्ते विशेषोऽयम् ॥६५॥

शब्दादिकगुणसाम्ये तुल्यत्वेनान्वयोऽत्र विज्ञेयः ।
अङ्गाङ्गिभावविहतो भवति विशेषस्तु केषाचित् ॥६६॥

तत्त्वानां स्थितिरेषा कथिता सृष्टिक्रमेण सर्वेषाम् ।
प्रतिलोमं परिणामात् मायायां तानि लीयन्ते ॥६७॥

मायायाः परतोऽप्य शुद्धः शक्तौ निलीयते सकलः ।
परमात्मनि सापि शिवे तिष्ठत्यविभागमापन्ना ॥६८॥

THE TATTVAPRAKĀŚA OF BHOJA PARAMĀRA | 117

<!-- page 117 src=20260817_163232.jpg -->

माया पुरुषः शिव इत्येतत्त्रितयं महार्थसंहारे ।
अवशिष्यते पुनस्तत्प्रवर्तते पूर्ववत् सृष्टौ ॥६६॥

संसारे खिन्नानां निखिलानां प्राणिनां प्रभुः कृपया ।
कुरुते महार्थसंहतिमेतेषामेव विश्रान्त्यै ॥७०॥

कर्मविपाचनहेतोः पशुदयया पुनरपीह परमेशः ।
सृष्टिं विधाय तेषां कर्म विपाचयति देहभूताम् ॥७१॥

भोगेन कर्मनाशं विधाय दीक्षाख्यया शक्त्या ।
मोचयति पशूनखिलान् करुणैकनिधिः सदा शम्भुः ॥७२॥

आप्रलयं यतिष्ठति सर्वेषां भोगदायि भूतानाम् ।
तत्त्वमिति प्रोक्तं न शरीरघटादि तत्त्वमतः ॥७३॥

तत्त्वं यतो भवेत् यत्कारणमापूरकं च तत्त्वस्य ।
कथिता व्यवस्थितिर्यन् निखिलानामेव तत्त्वानाम् ॥७४॥

तत्त्वानामपि तत्त्वं येनाखिलमेव लीलयां कथितम् ।
श्रीभोजदेवनृपतिर्व्यदधत्तत्त्वप्रकाशं सः ॥७५॥

यस्याखिलं करतलामलकक्रमेण
देवस्य विस्फुरुति चेतसि तत्त्वजातम् ।
श्रीभोजदेवनृपतिः स शिवागमार्थ
तत्त्वप्रकाशमसमानमिदं व्यधत्त ॥७६॥

<!-- page 118 src=20260817_163317.jpg -->

Tattvaprakāśa, one of the most significant works on Śaiva Siddhānta, was authored by King Bhoja Paramāra of Malwa (1000-1055 CE). It expounds the three principles of the Lord (pati), the individual soul (paśu) and the fetters (pāśa) in accordance with the Śaivāgamas. The paśus or the souls that go through the cycle of births and rebirths are regarded as belonging to different classes of bondage (pāśa). It is believed that when the impurities (mala) in a person get saturated, then god in the form of a preceptor would give proper initiation, so that the impurities may burn out and the souls so cleansed or purified may attain the nature of Śiva.
The Tattvaprakāśa has 75 kārikās which have all been translated literally. Any critical observations or critical comments on the verses have been written below the translated verse entitled 'note'. This note also takes into cognizance the detailed ṭīkā or commentary of Aghoraśivācārya and Śrīkumāradeva. Vṛtti is a commentary on Tattvaprakāśa by Aghoraśivācārya. Tātparyadīpikā is a commentary on Tattvaprakāśa by Śrīkumāradeva.

Verse 1: Śambhu excels all. He is entirely of the nature of consciousness, unique, all pervasive, ever manifest, the lord of all and quiescent, the only seed of the universe and gracious.

Note: Consciousness includes both the power of knowledge and the power of action.

Verse 2: That which is not born, that which is not destroyed, that which does not obtain salvation (itself) but bestows salvation, (such) is Śiva, who is the nature of both consciousness and will.

Note: The nature and power of Śiva is constituted by both knowledge and action (jñānakriyāsvabhāva). He creates a real world. We have here a dualistic and realistic picture.

Verse 3: I bow (to that power), Śakti, which is unequalled, foremost, conscious and which is the instrument by which Śiva is able to bestow pleasure and salvation to all beings.

Note: Śiva and Śakti are distinguishable but not separable.

Verse 4: For the welfare of the world and with compassion in my heart, the Tattvaprakāśa (Śaiva text) has been briefly compiled by me.

Note: This is for the ignorant beings of the world.

Verse 5: In the Śaivāgamas, the three (constituents) are respectively pati, paśu and pāśa. There (in the Āgamas), pati is said to be Śiva, paśu is aṇu or jīva and there are five pāśas.

Note: Arthānām malakarmasūkṣmasthūlamāyāpāśānugrāhakaśivaśaktīnām - pañcakamarthapañcakam, tatpāśākhyam tattvamiti. (Tātparyadīpikā on Tattvaprakāśa, verse 5)
Basically, however, there are only three pāśas, viz. mala, karma and māyā.

Verse 6: Though Śiva is himself liberated but the (jīvas) can only be liberated by his grace.
(Actually) he is perennially liberated, unique and his body consists of the five mantras.

THE TATTVAPRAKĀŚA OF BHOJA PARAMĀRA

119

<!-- page 119 src=20260817_163332.jpg -->

Note: Actually, what is meant is that the body of the deity itself can be aniconically worshipped in the form of the *mantra* body.

In his commentary, the *Tātparyadīpikā*, Śrīkumāra says that the *mantra* body itself has a five-fold aspect. These aspects are *nāda*, *bindu*, *pada*, *varṇa* and *vākya*.

*"pañcamantratanurīti nādabindupadavarṇavakyātmanā pañcātmakasakalamantratanīti ityarthah. Mantrasvarūpa iti vā. Nāda and bindu are inarticulate or avyadiyā dhvani. Bindu has a focus, it is the point from which the sound emerges and to which it returns like an echo. This echoing, reverberating character of sound is nāda. Pada is articulate sound. It is a word which has a meaning. Varṇa is its minimal aspect. It is a phoneme. Vākya is a sentence. It is a combination of words.*

The real meaning seems to be that the five *mantra* bodies are the five-fold aspects of Śiva. Śrīkumāra tries to drive home this fact by giving the parallel example of seven Vedic metres like, *gāyatrī*, *triṣṭubha*, *anuṣṭubha* etc. and identifying them with seven body elements like hair, skin, flesh etc; and even with *prāṇa*. In this manner, he tries to pave the way to comprehend as to how Śiva's body is made up of the *mantras*.

The *Vṛtti* also says that Śiva does not have any body in reality, nor faces, nor sensory organs. These are imputed to him only for the sake of the convenience of the mediator. Hence, when it is said that the body of Śiva is composed of five *mantras*, what is meant is that Śiva performs five functions and that the powers through which he functions are the constituents of his body. These powers are called the five *mantras* bodies and are named *Īśāna*, *Tatpurusa*, *Aghora*, *Vāmadeva* and *Sadyojāta*. These are also the five faces of Śiva.

Verse 7: Always said to be his five-fold activities are *sṛṣṭi* (creation), *sthiti* (maintenance) *saṃhāra* (destruction) *tirobhāva* (non-revealing or veiling) and *anugraha* (grace).

Note: *Tirobhāva* is the source of obscuration of true knowledge and hence of bondage.

This world as it is and its creation presupposes an intelligent cause. This intelligent cause is the lord, whose existence is proved by inference. The origin of theistic systems lies partly in the attempt to give an account of the world in terms of a creator who is also the source of moral law and the hope for human salvation. In the full-fledged Śaiva āgamic theistic systems, god is defined by five functions, viz. creation (*sṛṣṭi*), maintenance (*sthiti*), destruction of the world (*pralaya*), the source of obscuration or bondage (*tirodhāna*) and grace (*anugraha*). Different theistic systems define god with reference to these features in different measures. In particular, all believe that god is the source of creation and grace.

Verse 8: *Paśus* are known to be of three types viz; *vijñānākevala*, *pralayākevala* and *sakala*. The first is imbued with *mala* and the second with both *mala* and *karma*.

Verse 9: The *sakala* (*jīva*) carries with it the impurities of *mala*, *māyā* and *karma*. The first (i.e, *vijñānākevala*) is of two types, *samāptakaluṣa* and *asamāptakaluṣa*.

120

THE UDAYESVARA TEMPLE

<!-- page 120 src=20260817_163336.jpg -->

Verse 10: The top eight are appointed as Vidyeśvaras and others as (lord of) mantras and they are seven crores.

Note: These refer to the vijñānākala souls. In this process of purification some souls reach this state where they are completely freed from the bondage of karma and māyā, i.e, of the three bonds of māyā, karma and mala, the first two are completely extinguished. The vijñānākalas are divided into two categories. Those who are rid of the bond of mala (samāptakaluṣa) attain the state of liberation, and attain to a Śiva-like state of existence. Eight out these are elevated to the status of Vidyeśvaras, i.e. a deity like status. The rest who have the residue of some mala (asamāptakaluṣa) have to work out to get rid of their mala. They are then blessed by Śiva and attain to liberation and these are the presiding deities of the mantras. They are seven crores.

Verse 11: Of the pralayākalas, those whose mala and karma are destroyed, they are more (towards liberation). Others because of the force of their karmas enter into the subtle body called puryastaka.

Verse 12: This puryastaka is constituted by the antaḥkaraṇa (mind) and the senses of perception and action (jñāṇedriya and karmendriya). Some of these souls are blessed by Śiva to rule the different worlds.

Verse 13: The rest become sakala by their connection with the kalās at the dawn of time. Hundred and eighteen are appointed as Mantrēśas.

Verse 14: Of these, eight are Maṇḍalis, Krodha etc; are equivalent to them and then there are Vīreśa, Śrīkaṇṭha and the hundred Rudras. In this way, they are eighteen more than hundred.

Verse 15: Embodied in the figure of the teacher, he (Śiva) initiates those whose impurities have ripened (paripakvamala). Through such an initiation, he bestows grace and leads them to the highest truth.

Verse 16: The other souls who are bound are sent to experience the fruits of their action. They are called paśus because they follow the impulsions of their karmas.

Verse 17: Pāśas are of four types. The first two are born of mala and karma. The other two pāśas are māyā and tirodhāna born of Śiva and Śakti.

Verse 18: The mala covers the soul. Although it is one, it is of many kinds and it obscures the powers of knowledge and action. It is to be understood as the husk of rice or the patina on copper.

Verse 19: Karma is declared to be beginingless. It is of the nature of right and wrong and of strange varieties. Māyā is a something positive and is the source of creation.

Note: Māyā is actually the power of god and creation is not illusory. This is quite different from Advaita Vedānta or even Kashmir Śaivism.

THE TATTVAPRAKĀŚA OF BHOJA PARAMĀRA | 121

<!-- page 121 src=20260817_163343.jpg -->

Verse 20: The obscuring power of the lord is the support of the pāsas. The pāsas are so called because of their binding power and they are of four kinds.

Verse 21: There are five pure elements. Śiva is remembered as the original element. Śakti, Sadāśiva, Īśvara and Vidyā are the other (four) elements.

Verse 22: The five elements of māyā which limit the soul by ignorance and inactivity are kāla, niyati, kalā, vidyā and rāga.

Verse 23: From māyā proceeds the indeterminate (avyakta), from that the gunas (sattva, rajas and tamas), then cosmic intelligence (buddhi), then the principle of egoism (ahaṃkāra), then the senses of perception and action (jñānendriya, karmendriya and manas), then the five tanmātras or pure sense data (basic qualities which are perceived by the senses, like colour, sound, touch, taste, small etc), then proceed the five material elements (ether, air, fire, water and earth).

Verse 24: Twenty-three elements emanate from nature for the sake of awakening the spirit (puruṣa). There is no absolute difference between prakṛti and gunas.

Verse 25: The masters (ācāryas) have said that the Śiva tattva consists of jñānaśakti and kriyāśakti. It is all pervasive, non-dual, eternal and the cause of all elements.

Note: Śiva may not manifest his power. He also has a purely transcendent aspect, where all power or śakti is latent in him, i.e. Śiva in himself or by himself alone. Śiva has the mode of self expression or eternal expression or manifestation as omniscience or omnipotence. Then Śiva is not different from Śakti.

Verse 26: The powers of will etc, perform their specific function as part of this (Śiva tattva) and that is why Śiva is called the gracious supporter of all things.

Verse 27: Śiva is the cause of bestowing support and grace upon the sentient and insentient. When Śiva is about to create, his first shining forth (unmeṣa) or manifestation is the Śakti tattva which is not separate from him.

Note: Cit is jīva and acit is the world. Śiva in the mode of creativity is Śakti. This is the second moment of the śuddha addhvā.

Verse 28: When the Śaktis called jñāna and kriyā are in perfect balance, then this called Sadāśiva.

Verse 29: When the power of knowledge is less and the power of action exceeds it, then this is called Īśvara tattva, i.e, the creator of all things.

Verse 30: When kriyāśakti (power of action) becomes subordinate and jñānaśakti (power of knowledge) is dominant, then we have the Vidyā tattvā, which is luminous, being of the nature of knowledge.

122

THE UDAYEŚVARA TEMPLE

<!-- page 122 src=20260817_163347.jpg -->

Verse 31: Nāda and bindu along with kalā are dependent on the Sadāśiva tattva.
Vidyeśvaras are dependent on the Īśvara tattva and mantras and vidyās on
Vidyā tattva.

Note: Śrīkumāra says that in some systems nāda and bindu are mentioned separately.
It has to be classified whether these are included in these tattvas are not,
hence this verse above.

According to the Vṛtti, parābindu is included in Śiva tattva, nāda is included in
Śakti tattva and when they are associated with kalā they become akṣara bindu
and sthūlanāda, which is of the nature of sound, then they are identical with
Sadāśiva. What Aghoraśiva means is that Śiva, Śakti and Sadāśiva as three
moments of consciousness are characterized by supernatural light and sound
which are expressive of the knowledge and power of consciousness.

Verse 32: These five (of the śuddha addhvā) are not in any temporal succession because
there is no time at this level. These five are distinguished conceptually by their
functions.

Verse 33: There is, in reality, only one tattva, i.e. Śiva, but he has numerous powers in
him. These differences are constructed on account of the manifestations of
the various powers.

Verse 34: For the sake of grace for the sentient and the insentient, the lord creates these
different forms. He bestows his grace on the sentient souls whose powers
have been obscured by the force of beginningless mala.

Verse 35: To the individual souls he gives the power of experience of pleasure and pain
and also liberation. To the insentient he gives the powers to act within their
limits.

Verse 36: The grace on the sentient soul consists in their liberation which is the attainment
of a nature like that of Śiva. Because the actions are beginningless, liberation
is not possible without experiencing the result of actions.

Verse 37: For the sake of experience of the souls, the lord creates the bodies, senses and
the world of objects. There can be no action without the agent, the material
and the instruments.

Verse 38: His powers are the instruments and his māyā is the material cause (upādāna).
Māyā is subtle, eternal, without beginning and end, ubiquitous and śivāsakta
(dependent but not identical).

Verse 39: It (māyā) is common to all men, and the cause of the universe. Constituted by
the karma of all creatures, it is by nature the matrix of delusion (moha).

Verse 40: The lord, seeing the state of karmas, activates his powers and bestows on each
soul, bodies and sense appropriate for experiencing his karmas.

THE TATTVAPRAKĀŚA OF BHOJA PARAMĀRA | 123

<!-- page 123 src=20260817_163400.jpg -->

Verse 41: Constituted of many powers she (māyā) creates in the first instance time (kāsi) which is responsible for the world in the three dimensions of past, present and future.

Note: Iccḥādiḥaḥuvidhaśaktipreritā māyā janayati kālākhyam tattvam - Tātparyadiyā on Tattvaprakāśa, verse 41, i.e. "motivated by many powers such as desire, will etc, māyā creates time in the first instance.

Verse 42: Subsequently from māyā itself proceeds the power of determinism which regulates everything. This has been called niyati.

Note: According to Vṛtti, niyati is the force which ensures that an agent's experiences are determined by the force of his own action and not by the force of anyone else's action. Aghoraśiva gives the example of the case when in absence of government regulation, anyone might take away the benefit due to another. (Basically what he means is that if you sow a field another would not be able to reap it, or it you have to pay a debt another would not do it). The Vṛtti further says that two objections have been stated, the law of karman itself ensures it and the other that it is the power of Śiva that ensures it. One may note that the first is the objection of Buddhists and Jainas, the other of Vedāntists. The Vṛtti answers that the law of karman only ensures that every action produces an appropriate result. But since the result does not accrue immediately to the person (but may accrue after many lives) a further agency is required to connect the result of the action with the agent in the long seen. As for the answer to the second objection, it is that if everything is attributed to god's power directly then there is no place for any tattvas. How exactly is the regulation intended has been diversely understood. Niyati has been understood as causal and spatial determination. The Vṛtti explains the determination in terms of moral causality.

Verse 43: Then arises kalā, which, setting aside the force of mala to some extent sets free some power of action of the soul.

Note: The soul is covered fully by the original impurity (mala) which robs it of its natural power of knowledge and action. Kalā makes a dent in this covering and enables the soul to know and act in a limited manner. As a realistic and dualistic system, this philosophy believes that the individual soul does have some power of knowledge and will, so that it really acts in a real world. But this is a limited power, and, consequently, the knowledge and action of the soul is also imperfect and liable to error. Kalā is a part of the original power of the soul covered by mala. Kalā seems to be giving back some of this original power.

In the Advaita system, this would correspond to vikṣepa, i.e, what the soul sees and does in only in the background of illusion. So āvaraṇa and vikṣepa are the two aspects of māyā. In the example of the rope/snake, the true nature of the rope is covered by ignorance and the false snake is seen in it. The former is āvaraṇa and the latter is vikṣepa. However, some interpreters of Advaita, like Padmapadācārya, also belive avidyā to be bhāva-rūpa, i.e. it is not simple absence of knowledge but a positive state of illusion.

124

THE UDAYEŚVARA TEMPLE

<!-- page 124 src=20260817_163404.jpg -->

Verse 44: Subject to time and to subordinated *niyati*, it (*kalā*) performs her function constantly down to the element of earth.

Note: The *Vṛtti* says that: *kalā* is primary, being essential while time (*kāla*) and *niyati* are subordinated, being external. In other words, *kalā* is the essential power while time and *niyati* are its modalities.

Verse 45: *Kalā* produces *vidyā*. *Vidyā* produces the knowledge of objects of which some power of knowledge has been awakened by *kalā*.

Verse 46: This is the most important power of the self, which splits asunder the covering on the soul and enables it to perceive the world of objects. Therefore, *vidyā* is the best tool of the soul.

Verse 47: When *buddhi* itself is the object of experience as pleasure and pain, then *vidyā* is the tool. When *buddhi* knows external objects, then it itself is the tool.

Note: *Buddhi* is used in two senses. Sometimes it is the perception of external objects. Sometimes, it is itself an object of pleasure and pain. In either case, it is an instrument of knowledge for the soul.

*Yadā sattvarajastamoguṇātmikā buddhiḥ sukhadukhamohanarūpeṇa pariṇatā bhokturātmano bhogyā bhavati, tadeyam vidyā kāraṇam bhavet. Tātparyadīpikā* on *Tattvaprakāśa*, verse 47.

*Buddhi* has three constituents, *sattva*, *rajas* and *tamas*. When *sattva* dominates, it is pleasant, otherwise it is painful. *Buddhi* itself is transformed into pleasure or pain. It is the theory of Sāṃkhya that pleasure and pain are objective things but they are constituents of the mind, which means that whenever we know things, we know something of the nature of pleasure, pain or dullness.

Verse 48: *Rāga* is of the nature of attachment. It is common without the differentiation of objects (i.e. it is the same though objects may differ). It is the cause of the soul moving towards some objects. Its nature is different from the other properties or states of the mind.

Verse 49: When the *paśu* is led to the state of experiencing pleasure and pain by the force of these elements (*tattvas*), then he is called the *puruṣa* among the *tattvas* and is also counted as such amongst them.

Note: Śrīkumāra says that the idea is that the lord himself is bound by the various bonds of *māyā* and becomes a *paśu*!

*Yadāyamīśvaraḥ ebhiḥ kālaniyatikalāvidyārāgākhyaiḥ pañcabhistattvaiḥ sambandhāt paśubhāvam prāpyā bhoktṛtvāvasthām prāponti tadā vyaktādicaturviṃśatitattvamaye puri śayanāt puruṣasañjñām ca labhate. Tātparyadīpikā* on *Tattvaprakāśa*, verse 49.

The *Vṛtti*, however, contradicts this. Actually, this comment of Śrīkumāra would be correct only on an *advaitika* supposition. There is no support in the text for this. In fact, the text clearly says that when the *paśu* becomes a *bhoktā*, he is called *puruṣa*.

Verse 50: For the sake of the *bhoga* of the *puruṣa*, *kalā* produces the *avyakta* or unmanifest. This *avyakta* cannot be conceptualized or named because its qualities are unmanifest.

THE TATTVAPRAKĀŚA OF BHOJA PARAMĀRA | 125

<!-- page 125 src=20260817_163413.jpg -->

Note: Bhoga means experience of pleasure and pain. Avyakta is similar to the avyakta of Sāṃkhya which is the unmanifest matrix of the world and is constituted by the three guṇas but these guṇas are not manifested in this state. The motivating force behind the creation of nature is kalā. The doctrine is similar to the Sāṃkhya doctrine.

Verse 51: From the unmanifest proceeds the manifestation of the guṇas. These guṇas are of the nature of revelation or manifestation, motion or action, and of the nature of inhibition of motion, i.e. inertia. These are also called sattva, rajas and tamas and also pleasure, pain and dullness.

Verse 52: Out of the three guṇas arises intelligence which determines the nature of objects. That is described as three fold in accordance with the predominance of guṇa and karma.

Note: Out of the darkness of the unmanifest, the first creation begins with light, which is not merely physical light but essentially the light of intelligence. Cosmic intelligence, impersonal and infinite, containing all the principles of knowledge, is the first creation. Just as a man intending to make something first thinks it out, similarly, the ideal forms of things are manifested in the principle of cosmic intelligence. Its specific character in the individual soul, however, differs in accordance with his karma.

Verse 53: Ahaṃkāra is of three kinds, viz, vitality, conation and egoism. In its presence, objects become objects of experience.

Note: Further classification of ahaṃkāra into three is part of this system. Actually, the second line implies the fourth and the deepest aspect of ahaṃkāra, i.e. subjectivity. Since ahaṃkāra is the principle of subjectivity, external objects become the objects of experience and, thus, experience is made possible. Ahaṃkāra means the self as the vital principle engaged in effort as desire and will and as self reference, it is also the subject of experience. In other words, ahaṃkāra is another name for the empirical self.

Verse 54: That ahaṃkāra is of three varities, viz, sāttvika, rājasika and tāmasika and is called vaikārika, taijas and bhūtādi.

Note: Śrīkumāra explains it thus: sa punarahaṃkāro vaikārikataijasa bhūtādikanāmabhistrividho bhavatīti. Sattvoḍṛktaḥ sāttviko vaikārikākhyah. Rājodṛktastaijasaḥ. Kecid vaiparītyamicchanti sa ca taijasavaikārikabhūtādiketi granthapāṭhaśca ityāhuḥ. Tamoḍṛktaḥ tāmasaḥ. Sa bhūtādi nāmeti guṇato nāmataśca traividhyamahaṃkārsya bhavatīti. Tātparyadīpikā on Tattvaprakāsa, verse 54.

Ahaṃkāra is of three types called vaikārika, taijasika and bhūtādi. Where sattva is predominant it is called vaikārika, where rajasa is predominant it is called taijasika and where tamasa is predominant, it is called bhūtādi. Śrīkumāra says that some texts wish to reverse vaikārika and taijasika.

Verse 55: Vaikārika are the sense organs (both jñānedriya and karmendriya), taijas is the mind (manas) and bhūtādi is the sense data; all these arise from ahaṃkāra.

126

THE UDAYEŚVARA TEMPLE

<!-- page 126 src=20260817_163418.jpg -->

Note: The mind, senses and *tanmātras* are born of *ahaṅkāra*. Thus, the organs of knowledge, the organs of action and the organs of sense data, these are the three types of evolutes that arise out of *ahaṅkāra*.

Verse 56: Mind (*manas*) is of the nature of desire and it functions by a volition. These senses of cognition are eyes, ears nose, tongue and touch.

Verse 57: The objects of cognition are sound (*śabda*), touch (*sparśa*), form (*rūpa*), taste (*rasa*) and smell (*gandha*). These are the objects in order of the five senses.

Verse 58: The senses apprehend sound etc. Tongue, hands, feet, the organs of excretion and procreation are the senses of action (*karmendriya*).

Verse 59: Speaking, taking hold or grasping, walking, excretion and procreation are the objects of the five senses of action. *Antaḥkaraṇa* (inner sense) is three fold, viz. *buddhi*, *ahaṅkāra* and *manas*.

Note: The *antaḥkaraṇa* or the *citta* or inner sense differs according to the difference of *puruṣa*. This *antaḥkaraṇa* is the inner organ which coordinates, all the sense activities. It has three names in accordance with the three fold nature of its functioning. It is *ahaṅkāra* when in an egoistic mode, it is *buddhi*, when in a mode of judgment and determination and *manas* when it reflects or doubts (*saṅkalpa-vikalpa*).

Verse 60: The *antaḥkaraṇa* becomes ten-fold with references to the senses of knowledge and action. From the five sense data arise the five material elements, i.e. ether, air, *tejas* (*agni* or energy of heat or fire and light), water and earth.

Verse 61: The five *tanmātras* are the non-particular forms of sound etc. The material elements arise out them, each having one additional quality.

Note: *Ākāśa* (ether) has only one quality, i.e, *śabda* or sound.
*Vāyu* (air) has the qualities of sound as also touch (*sparśa*).
*Agni* (fire) has the qualities of sound, touch and form (*rūpa*).
*Jala* (water) has the qualities of sound, touch form and taste (*rasa*).
*Pṛthvī* (earth) has the qualities of sound, touch, form, taste and smell (*gandha*).

Verse 62: The function of these material elements in order are to give space or *avakāśa* (function of ether), to organise or order, heating (function of *tejas*), cohesion (function of water) and supporting (function of earth).

Note: The function of air is given to be motion everywhere. As such, it should be *īraṇa* or *guti*. Why *vyūhana* is written is not clear. *Vyūhana* means a 'specifically organised whole'. This is actually the function of water.

Verse 63: The ten-fold senses act through the help of the *tanmātras* in the *bhūtas*, because they are in themselves not capable of acting independently.

Note: The senses need material organs for their functions.

THE TATTVAPRAKĀŚA OF BHOJA PARAMĀRA | 127

<!-- page 127 src=20260817_163435.jpg -->

Verse 64: In Śaiva (Śiddhānta) the first five (of the *śuddha addhvā*) are connected by being of the nature of consciousness. But the connections of seven *tattvas* beginning with *māyā* is said to be two-fold, through sentience and through insentience.

Note: Seven *tattvas* are *māyā*, five *kañuckas* and *puruṣa*.

Verse 65: The material world created from the *avyakta* is constituted by the three *puruṣa* of the nature of *sukha*, *dukha* and *moha* (insentiency).

Note: According to *Vṛtti*, they are first connected by the fact that limited knowledge etc. belong to *puruṣa*. This is the connection through sentience. At the same time, the support of this is given by the subtle body and that is the insentient connection. He also says that *anvaya* is a concept used in Śaiva Śiddhānta in place of *jāti* (*jāti* is a universal feature). Instead of accepting this universal feature, as in *jāti*, they prefer the term *anvaya*. Basically *anvaya* means common feature (*anugata dharma*). So in *śuddha addhvā* consciousness is the common feature. Here in the *aśuddha addhvā* from *māyā* to *puruṣa*, we have the *puruṣa* with a limited consciousness and a subtle body. So both of these provide common features of *māyā*, *kañuckas* and *puruṣa*.

Verse 66: The connection here in the first place is through the similarities in qualities like *śabda* etc. Some others have the connection of part and whole.

Note: The sense data and the material elements are thus connected by the similarity of qualities like sound etc. The elements from *avyakta* down to *tanmātras* are connected by the cause-effect and whole-part relations.

Verse 67: In the order of creation these *tattvas* have been mentioned. In the courses of dissolution of all these will be dissolved in *māyā*.

Verse 68: The way beyond *māyā* dissolves in Śakti in the *śuddha addhvā*. In Śiva, the supreme self, Śakti remains as an undivided reality.

Verse 69: In the great dissolution or *pralaya* three entities remains *māyā*, *puruṣa* and Śiva. In creation, they act as before.

Note: The *Vṛtti* explains that *māyā* here means *māyā* as well as *mahāmāyā*, the two material causes. *Puruṣa* means all the souls bound with impurities. Śiva means Śiva and Śakti and the liberated souls.

Śrīkumāra says that to make creation possible anew these three elements remain.

Actually, the whole process of creation and dissolution has these three permanent elements.

Verse 70: The lord, out of compassion for all the creatures weary of *saṃsāra*, gathers into dissolution the great principles of bondage (*kāla* etc).

Verse 71: Then again, the supreme lord, for the sake of the maturation of *karma*, out of compassion for the *paśus* having created the world, helps the creatures in the maturation of their *karmas*.

128

THE UDAYEŚVARA TEMPLE

<!-- page 128 src=20260817_163439.jpg -->

Verse 72: Sambhu, who is the unique treasure of compassion, liberates the patus by his power called dikṣā or initiation after annihilating their karma by arranging for the experience of its consequences.

Verse 73: That which endures till the dissolution of the universe and provides the results of karma to all living beings, that is called tattva. Hence, the bodies or (composite things like) jars are not tattvas.

Note: Tattvas are essential elements which endure despite transformations. They are different from composite products which are insentient, natural or artificial.

Verse 74: That from which the tattva arises, that which is the cause and compliment of the tattvas and the system of all the tattvas, this has been stated.

Verse 75: The tattva of the tattvas has also been stated with ease by King Bhoja who has composed the Tattvaprakāśa.

Verse 76: That king, in whose mind the full reality (Śaivāgama) was disclosed in the manner of an āmalaka* placed on the palm,* he was king Śrī Bhojadeva, who for the sake of Śaivāgama composed the incomparable text called the Tattvaprakāśa.

* Fruit of the Emblic Myrobalan.

+ Hasāmalakam literally means 'to hold an āmalaka fruit in the palm'. It is a well known Sanskrit

idiom which means to know a thing completely in all its aspects.
THE TATTVAPRAKĀŚA OF BHOJA PARAMĀRA | 129

# Appendix

# SAMARĀṄGAṆASŪTRADHĀRA OF BHOJA PARAMĀRA

# Chapter 77

अथ देवादिरूपप्रहरणसंयोगलक्षणं नाम सप्तसप्ततितमोऽध्यायः ।

त्रिदशानामथाकारान् ब्रूमः प्रहरणानि च ।

दैत्यानामथ यक्षाणां गन्धर्वोरगरक्षसाम् ॥१॥

विद्याधरपिशाचानां ++++ यथायथम् ।

ब्रह्मानलाचिःप्रतिमः कर्तव्यः सुमहाद्युतिः ॥२॥

स्मूलाङ्गः श्वेतषुष्पश्च श्वेतवेष्टनवेष्टितः ।

कृष्णजिनोत्तरीयश्च श्वेतवासाश्चतुर्मुखः ॥३॥

दण्डः कमण्डलुश्चास्य कर्तव्यो वामहस्तयोः ।

अक्षमूत्रधरस्तद्वा(द्वद्) मौञ्ज्या मेखलया वृतः ॥४॥

कार्या(यौ) वर्धयमानस्तु जगद् दक्षिणपाणिना ।

एवं कृते तु लोके(शे) क्षेमं भवति सर्वतः ॥५॥

ब्राह्मणा(र्थ) वर्धन्ते सर्वकामैर्न संशयः ।

यदा विरूपा (दीनां वा कूरसोदरी ॥६॥

ब्राह्मणैर्वा?) भवेद् वर्णा(?) सा नेष्टा भयदायिनी ।

निहन्ति कारकं रौद्रा दीनरूपा च शिल्पिनम् ॥७॥

कृसा व्याधि(धिं) विनाशं च कुर्यात् कारयितुः सदा ।

कृशोदरी तु दुर्भिक्षं विरूपा चानपत्यताम् ॥८॥

APPENDIX | 131

<!-- page 130 src=20260817_163526.jpg -->

एतान् दोषान् परित्यज्य कर्तव्या सा सुशोभना ।
ब्रह्मणोवा(ऽर्चा) विधानज्ञैः प्रथमो(मे) यौवने स्थिता ॥९॥

चन्द्राङ्कितजटः श्रीमान् नीलकण्ठः सुसंयते(तः) ।
विचित्रमुकुटः शम्भुनिशाकरसमप्रभः ॥१०॥

दोभ्यां द्वाभ्यां चतुर्भिंवा (वधा?) युक्तो वा दोभिरष्टभिः ।
पटि (टि)शव्यग्रहस्तश्च पन्नगाजिनसंयुतः ॥११॥

सर्वलक्षणसम्पूर्णो नेत्रत्रितयभूषणः ।
एवंविधगुणैरुक्तो यत्र लोकेश्वरो हरः ॥१२॥

परा तत्र भवेद् वृद्धिदेशस्य च नृपस्य च ।
यदारण्ये (समाने?) वा विधीयेत महेश्वरः ॥१३॥

एवं रूपस्तदा कार्यः कारकस्य शुभावहः ।
अष्टादशभुलो(जो) दोष्णां विशत्या वा समन्वितः ॥१४॥

शतबाहुः कदाचिद्वा सहस्रभुज एव च ।
रौद्ररूपो गणवृतः सिंहचर्मोत्तरीयकः ॥१५॥

तीक्ष्णदंष्ट्राग्रदशनः शिरोमालाविभूषितः ।
चन्द्राङ्कितशिराः श्रीमान् पीनोरस्कोगदर्शनः ॥१६॥

(भद्रम्?) कर्तव्यः श्मशानस्थो महेश्वरः ।
द्विभुजो राजधान्यां तु पत्तने स्याच्चतुर्भुजः ॥१७॥

कर्तव्यो विशतिभुजः श्मशानारण्यमध्यगः ।
एकोऽपि भगवान् भद्र(ः) स्थानभेदविकल्पितः ॥१८॥

रौद्रसौम्यस्वभावश्च क्रियमाणो भवेद् बुधैः ।
(उद्याद्यथा भावद्वागभगवान्) सौम्यदर्शनः ॥१९॥

स एव तीक्ष्णतामेति मध्यन्दिनगतः पुनः ।
तथारण्यस्थितो नित्यं रौद्रो भवति शङ्करः ॥२०॥

(स येद सौम्यावति स्थाने सौम्यो व्यवस्थितः?) ।
स्थानान्येतानि सर्वाणि ज्ञात्वा किम्पुरुषादिभिः ॥२१॥

प्रमथैः सहितः कार्यः शङ्करो लोकशङ्करः ।
एतद् यथावत् कथितं संस्थानं त्रिपुरदुहः ॥२२॥

कार्तिकेयस्य संस्थानमिदानीमभिधीयते ।
तरूणार्कनिभो रक्तवासाः भावकसप्रभः ॥२३॥

<!-- page 131 src=20260817_163539.jpg -->

ईषद्वालाकृतिः कान्तो मङ्गल्यः प्रियदर्शनः ।
प्रसन्नवदनः श्रीमानोजस्तेजोन्नितः शुभः ।।२४।।

(विशेषान्मुदुकैश्चित्रि?) मुक्तामणि(वि) भूषितः ।
षण्मुखो वैकवक्त्रो वा शक्तिं रोचिष्मतीं दधत् ।।२५।।

नगरे द्वादशभुजः खेटके षड्भुजो भवेत् ।
ग्रामे भुजद्वयोपेतः कर्तव्यः शुभमिच्छता ।।२६।।

शक्तिः शरस्तथा खड्गो मुसृण्ठी मुद्ररोऽपि च ।
हस्तेषु दक्षिणेष्वेतान्यायुधान्यस्य दर्शयेत् ।।२७।।

एकः प्रसारितश्चान्यः षष्ठो हस्तः प्रकीर्तितः ।
(चतुः?) पताका घण्टा च खेटः कुकुट(क) स्तथा ।।२८।।

वामहस्तेषु षष्ठस्तु तत्र (शोजर्जन?) करः ।
एवमायुधसम्पन्नः संग्रामस्थो विधीयते ।।२९।।

(अव्यया) तु विधातव्यः क्रीडालीलान्वितश्च सः ।
छागकुक्कुटसंयुक्तः शिखियुक्तो मनोरमः ।।३०।।

नगरेषु सदा कार्यः स्कन्दः परजयैषिभिः ।
खेटके तु विधातव्यः षण्मुखो ज्वलनप्रभः ।।३१।।

तथा तीक्ष्णायुधोपेतः स्रगदामभिरलङ्कृतः
ग्रामेऽपि द्विभुजः कार्यः कान्तिद्युतिसमन्वितः ।।३२।।

(दक्षिणा च भवेद् भक्तिर्नाम हस्ते तु कुक्कुटः?) ।
विचित्रपक्षः स(सु) महान् कर्तव्योऽतिमनोहरः ।।३३।।

एवं पुरे खेटके च ग्रामे (वाभिलं?) शुभम् ।
कार्तिकेयं ++ कुर्यादाचार्यः शास्त्रकोविदः ।।३४।।

अविरुद्धेषु कार्येषु खेटे या(ग्रा) मे पुरोत्तमे ।
कार्तिकेयस्य संस्थानमेतद् यत्रेन कारयेत् ।।३५।।

(बालस्तु सुभुजः श्रीमान् स्थले केतु महाद्युतिः?) ।
वनमालाकुलोरस्को निशाकरसमप्रभः ।।३६।।

गृहीतसारो(सीर) मुसलः कार्यो दिव्यमदोत्कटः ।
चतुर्भुजः सौम्यवक्रो नीलाम्बरसमावृतः ।।३७।।

कु(मु) कुटालङ्कृतशिरारोहो रागविभूषितः ।
रेवतीसहितः कार्यो वन (बल) देवः प्रतापवान् ।।३८।।

विष्णुवैदूर्यसङ्काशः पीतवासाः श्रियाक्(वृ) तः ।

वराहो वामनश्च स्यान्तरसिंहो भयानकः ।।३९।।

कार्यो (वा ?) दशरथी रामो जामदग्न्यश्च वीर्यवान् ।

द्विभुजोऽष्टभुजो वापि चतुर्बाहुररिन्दमः ।।४०।।

शङ्खचक्रगदापाणिरोजस्वी कान्तिसंयुतः ।

नानारूपस्तु कर्तव्यो ज्ञात्वा कार्यान्तरं विभुः ।।४१।।

इत्येषु विष्णुः कथितः (सुरास्वरनमस्वरनमस्त्वतः ?) ।

त्रिदशेशः सहस्राक्षौ(क्षो) वज्रभृत् सुभुजो बली ।।४२।।

किरीटी सगदः श्रीमाज् श्वेताम्बरधरस्तथा ।

श्रोणिसूत्रेण महा(हता) दिव्याभरणभूषितः ।।४३।।

कार्यो राजश्रिया युक्तः पुरोहितसहायवान् ।

वैवस्वतस्तु विज्ञेयः (काले: केसं ?) परायणः ।।४४।।

तेजसा सूर्यसङ्काशो जाम्बूनदविभूषितः ।

सम्पूर्णचन्द्रवदनः पीतवासास्तु(ःशु) भेक्षणः ।।४५।।

विचित्रमुकुटः कार्यो वराङ्गदविभूषितः ।

तेजसा सूर्यसङ्काशः कर्तव्यो बलवाञ्छुभः ।।४६।।

धन्वन्तरिर्भरद्वाजः (प्रजानीयतयस्तथा ।

दक्षार्थाः सदृशाः कार्या कार्यो रूपाणि+रपि ?) ।।४७।।

अर्चिष्मान् क्ष(ज्व)लनः कार्यः(यत्कण्ठाश्व ?)समीरणः ।

सौम्यः कार्यस्तथा(विस्या ?) ++ रुद्रशरीरिणः ।।४८।।

रक्तवस्त्रधराः कृष्णा नानाभरणभूषिताः ।

कर्तव्या राक्षसाः सर्वे बहुप्रहरणान्विताः ।।४९।।

पूर्णचन्द्रमुखो शुभ्रा बिम्बोष्ठी चारूहासिनी ।

श्वेतवस्त्रधरा कान्ता दिव्यालङ्कारभूषिता ।।५०।।

कटिदेशनिविष्टेन वामहस्तेन शोभना ।

सपद्येन (वान्तेन ?) दक्षिणेन शुचिस्मिता ।।५१।।

कर्तव्या श्रीः प्रसन्नास्या प्रथमे यौवने स्थिता ।

गृहीतशूलपरिघ(पाहिका ?) पट्टिसध्वजा ।।५२।।

बिभ्राणा खेटकोपेतलघुखङ्गं च पाणिना ।

घण्टामेकां च सौवर्णां दधती घोररूपिणी ।।५३।।

कौशिकी पीतकौशेयवसना सिंहवा(ह)ना ।
(सेचोही?)+विधातव्याः शुकाम्बरधराः ++।।५४।।

शोभमानाश्च मुकुटैर्नानारद्वविभूषितैः।
सहशावान्वनी कार्यो लोकस्य शुभदायकौ।।५५।।

शुकमाल्याम्बरधरौ जाम्बूनदविभूषितौ ।
(त्रिपञ्चदशपूतिरस्येदं भृङ्गवन्मेचकप्रभाम् ।।५६।।

वैदूर्यशकंसङ्काशा?) हरितशमश्रवोऽपि च।
रोहिता विकृता रक्तलोचना बहुरूपिणः ।।५७।।

नागैः शिरोरूहालीनैर्विरागाभरणाम्बराः ।
कार्या: पिशाचा भूताश्च परुषासत्यवादिनः ।।५८।।

(बहुप्रकारमन्दहा?) विरूपा विकृताननाः।
घोररूपा विधातव्या हस्वा नानासु(यु)धाश्च ते।।५९।।

सुभीमविक्रमा भीमा(:) सङ्घा यज्ञोपवीतिनः ।
वर्मभिः शाटिकाचित्रैर्भूताः कार्या: सदा बुधैः ।।६०।।

येऽपि नोक्ता विधातव्यास्तेऽपि कार्यानुरूपतः ।
यस्य यस्य च यज्ञिङ्गमसुरस्य सुरस्य च ।।६१।।

यक्षराक्षसयोर्वापि नाना(ग) गन्धर्वयोरपि।
तेन लिङ्गेन कार्यः स यथा साशु(धु) विजानजा(ता)।।६२।।

प्रायेण (वा?) वीर्यवन्तो हि दानवाः क्रूरकर्मिणः।
किरीटिनश्च कर्तव्या विविधायुधपाणयः ।।६३।।

तेभ्योऽपीषत् कनीयांसो दैत्याः कार्या गुणैरपि।
दैत्येभ्यः परिहीणास्तु यक्षाः कार्या मदोत्कटाः ।।६४।।

हीनास्तेभ्योऽपि गन्धर्वा गन्धर्वेभ्योऽपि पन्नगाः ।
नागेभ्यो राक्षसा हीनाः क्रूर(विक्रिमतसूषिणः ?) ।।६५।।

विद्याधराश्च यक्षेभ्यो हीनदेहत(ध)राः स्मृताः ।
चित्रमाल्याम्बरधराश्चित्रचर्मासिपाणयः ।।६६।।

नानावेषधरा घोरा भूतसङ्घा भयानकाः ।
पिशाचेभ्योऽधिकाः स्थूलास्तेजसा परुषास्तथा ।।६७।।

अन्युनाधिकरूपांश्च कुर्वीत प्रायशः शुभान् ।
(दिव्यैरासणाभरणैश्च युक्ताः
कृतीयविदधीत यथोदितांस्तान्) ।।६८।।

# SAMARĀṄGAṆASŪTRADHĀRA OF BHOJA PARAMĀRA

## Chapter 77

1-2. Now we describe the forms of the gods and also their weapons: of the daityas, yakṣas, gāndharvas, nāgas, rakṣasas, vidyādharas and piśācas as they are. Brahmā should be made like a flame of fire (analārcīḥpratīmaḥ) greatly effulgent.
3. Of stout limbs, having white flowers (or flower), with a white headdress, he (Brahmā) has an upper garment of black antelope skin, a white garment and four faces.
4. In the left hand should be fashioned the staff and the water-pot. He should also have a rosary and be encircled with a girdle of munja (grass).
5. His right hand should be blessing* the universe. If the lord of the universe is fashioned in this way it leads to the well-being of the whole world.
6. The brahmanas have all their desires fulfilled, there is no doubt (i.e. if the image of Brahmā is well made). When ill-made and miserable-looking (it seems to have dire consequences).
7. From Brahmā are all the varnas created. Such an image of Brahmā should not be terrible-looking. If the image is malevolent, or fierce-looking it will kill the patron. If it is miserable-looking it will kill the sculptor.
8. If (the image) is (too) slender, it will always cause disease and destruction to the patron. If it has (too) slender an abdomen, it will cause famine. If it is malformed, it will lead to childlessness.
9. Avoiding these faults, the image should be made splendid. The image of Brahmā for worshipping should be in the first flush of youth according to the masters of prescriptions.
10. Śiva has matted locks marked by the moon, is glorious, dark-throated, with a well-balanced picturesque tiara and luminous as the moon.
11. Having two, four or eight arms, holding a paṭṭiśa (kind of spear with sharp edge), restlessly in the hands, he carries a snake and antelope skin.
12. Endowed with all the characteristic signs, ornamented with three eyes, imbued with such qualities should the image of Lokeśvara Śiva be made.
13. This will lead to the ultimate success of the country and the king when the image of Maheśvara is made in the forest or cremation ground.
14. Thus should the image be made for the good of the patron, either with eighteen or twenty arms.
15. It could possible be with a hundred arms or a thousand, with a fierce aspect, surrounded by gaṇas or attendants, with an upper garment of tiger skin.
16. With sharp teeth and jaws, with a garland ornamenting the head, with the head marked by the moon, he is splendid with a muscular (stout) chest and a fierce aspect.
17. The image of Śiva in the cremation ground should be with a benign aspect. In the capital town, the image should be of two arms and in a port town of four arms.

* The literal translation is 'increasing'. Here it seems to refer to varada, blessing and boons-giving to the universe.

+ Emendation suggests śmaśāne which seems correct.

This is following the emendation of the editor that bhadramū should be bhadramūrtistu.

136

THE UDAYEŚVARA TEMPLE

<!-- page 135 src=20260817_163603.jpg -->

18. It should be of twenty arms in the cremation ground and in the middle of the forest. The lord is one and benign but diversely imaged in different places.

19. He would be fierce or benign as he is made by those who know. He would be benign in aspect as the rising sun.*

20. Just as the sun becomes fierce at the time of midday, similarly Śaṅkara becomes fierce when he is in the forest.

21. In a benign place he is ordained as benign. (These images should be made) after knowing all these places from the kimpuruṣas⁺ (sylvan spirits).

22. Śaṅkara who is benign for the world should be made with his gaṇas. Thus, the form of the enemy of Tripura (Śiva) has been described properly.

23. Now is being stated the form of Kārttikeya. It should be like the rising sun and have red garments like the fire.

24. His form should be somewhat child like, attractive, auspicious, pleasant looking, with a cheerful countenance, splendid, endowed with energy and vitality and beautiful.

25. Ornamented with a special variegated tiara, adorned with pearls (pearls and gems), he has six faces or one and carries a shining spear.

26. In the town (he is depicted) with twelve arms, in the kheṭaka (small village) with six arms, in the village with two arms. For those who want well-being, he should be fashioned like this.

27. In the right hands (should be shown) the following weapons, viz. spear, arrow, sword, musṛṇṭhī and mudgara (club or mace).

28-29. One arm, the sixth, is said to be extended. In the left hands should be shown the bow, flag, shield and cock. The sixth hand should be shown as blessing.ᵒ Thus endowed with weapons, he is shown as in battle.

30. At other times, he should be shown in a playful form. He should be shown with a goat (chāga) and a cock and with a beautiful peacock.

31. Those who want victory over others should establish the image of Skanda in their towns. In the kheṭaka, he should be fashioned with six faces radiant like the fire.

32. He should be endowed with sharp weapons and ornamented with garlands. In the village, he should be made with two arms imbued with an attractive and glowing appearance.

33. In the right hand should be a spear⁺, in the left a cock (emendation followed). It should be fashioned with variegated feathers, of large size and fascinating.

34. These should be made in the town, district and village. (These are) benign images of Kārttikeya by the master who is well-versed in the śāstras.

35. When affairs are moving smoothly, this form of Kārttikeya should be made with great care in district, village and capital towns.

36. Bala should be made with beautiful arms, splendid, with a flag of great brilliance, with the vanamālā on his chest and radiant like the moon.

37. He should hold a plough-share, a musala or pestle and be forceful by celestial intoxicants. He has four arms, benign countenance and covered by a blue garment.

* Following the emendation.

⁺ Amarakośa. 1.1.71. Kimpuruṣastu kinnaraḥ

ᵒ Emendation has been followed. Śojarjana has been emended to sanivardhana.

⁺ Bhaktirvāmahāste is an error and should be replaced by śaktirvāmahāste.

APPENDIX

137

<!-- page 136 src=20260817_163612.jpg -->

38. His hair decorated with a tiara, he should be flushed with colour and should be accompanied with Revatī. Thus should be Baladeva of great prowess.

39. Viṣṇu has the complexion of blue sapphire (*vaidūrya*), has yellow garments and is accompanied by Śrī. Varāha, Vāmana or Narasiṁha have a fearsome aspect.

40. Rāma, the son of Daśaratha and the son of Jāmadagni are to be made heroic. They are to be made with two arms, eight arms or four arms and overcoming the enemy.

41. Having in his hands the conch, discus and mace, vigorous and brilliant, (Viṣṇu is) to be made in diverse forms after knowing what else is to be done.

42-43. Thus is Viṣṇu described to whom obeisance is made by gods and demons. The lord of the gods has a thousand eyes, bears the thunderbolt has well-made *kirīṭamukuṭa* and a mace. He is lustrous, wears white clothes, a waistband and is adorned with large and celestial ornaments.

44. Thus, (he) should he be made with royal splendour and accompanied by a priestly attendant Vaivasvata. However, it is to be known by...

45. He should be with radiance like the sun, adorned with gold, with a face like the full moon, with yellow garments and with beautiful eyes.

46. With a variegated diadem, adorned with a precious armlet, with radiance like that of the sun, full of prowess and auspicious (should he be).

47. Dhanvantri, Bhāradvāja ...* Dakṣa and others’ should be made similarly with varied forms.

48. The fire god should be made with flames and the wind god should be made...° He is to be made benevolent...(text missing), of fearsome aspect.†

49. Wearing red garments, dark complexioned, wearing various ornaments, all the demons are to be made thus and armed with weapons.

50-52. With a face like the full-moon, brilliant, lips like the *bimba* fruit, smiling beautifully, wearing white garments, graceful adorned with celestial ornaments, with the left hand placed on the waist, beautiful, with a lovely lotus in the right hand, with a gleaming smile, thus should be made Śrī, with a cheerful countenance and in the first flush of youth.

52-55. Carrying a lance, a club studded or tipped with iron (*parigha*), a sharp edged spear (*paṭṭisa*), banner, also bearing the shield and small sword, bell made of gold, with a fearsome aspect, wearing garments of yellow silk, seated on a lion should be made Kauśikī.§

55-56. The two Āśvins should be made similar (they are twins), auspicious to the world, wearing white clothes and garlands and decked with gold. (The *pretas*) are blue and dark like the bees.*

57. They are like the blue sapphire and also lustrous like the green stone (presumably emerald). They are red, malformed, with red eyes, assuming several forms.

* Reading is defective.

+ Dakṣārthā is probably a corruption of Dakṣādyā. The reference here seems to be to the Vasus, including Dakṣa, Bhāga etc.

□ Reading not clear.

† *Gaṇa devatā* included benevolent gods like the Vasus and fearsome gods like the Rudras.

§ The predicates of the next two lines are “should be made wearing white garments brilliant with diadems, adorned with gems”, but the subject (which seems to be in plural) is not clear.

The first half of the second line is not clear.

138

THE UDAYEŚVARA TEMPLE

<!-- page 137 src=20260817_163616.jpg -->

58. With snakes lurking in their hair, with clothes and ornaments of faded colour; so should be made the piśācas and bhūtas who speak harshly and falsely.
59. Of many varieties, of dull (appearance)* malformed with distorted faces, fearsome forms, they should be made short-statured with diverse weapons.
60. With terrible prowess, terrifying, in troupes, with sacred thread, the wise always make the bhūtas (wearing) skins which are variegated with the cloth of śāṭikās.†
61-62. Those who are not mentioned here should be made in accordance with their proper from (anurūpataḥ).° Whatever is the sign (linga) of a god or titan (asura) or any yakṣa, rākṣasa nāga or gandharva, by that sign, should its image be made in accordance with right knowledge.
63. By and large the dānavas are characterized by power (vīryavantaḥ) addicted to cruel deeds. They should be made wearing diadems and having various weapons in their hands.
64. Some what younger than the dānavas should be made the daityas, so also in respect of their virtues. Inferior to the daityas should be made the yakṣas who should (appear) exuberant with intoxication.
65. Inferior to the yakṣas are the gandharvas and inferior to these are the nāgas. Inferior to the nāgas are the rākṣasas who are cruel and deformed.†
66. Vidyādhara are considered lower than yakṣas in the scale of bodies. They have splendid garlands and clothes and splendid swords and shield in their hands.
67. The troupes of bhūtas are dressed in various ways, have a terrible aspect and are larger than the piśācas. They are stout and harsh by their aura (of power).
68. They should be made neither more nor less in their forms and generally good-looking, and they should have celestial ornaments.

* Word not clear. Is it mandākhā or mandāsyā?

† This would mean a cloth, a garment or petticoat.

° Every object in the world has its own proper form (rūpabhedaḥ). That is why it has been said that śilpa is pratirūpa. The idea is that śilpa is the imaging of the proper essential form. Rūpa is reminiscent of the Platonic Eidos.

† Emendation followed.
