45. He has the lustrous beauty of pearls and has the radiance of molten gold, he is imbued with necklace and armlet and is adorned by a halo.
46. He is three-eyed, six armed, fair, matted locks adorned with a digit of the moon, wears a tiger skin, white, benign and has a snake sacred thread.
47. With a trident, rosary, skull, water-pot in the right hands and in the other benign hands he carries snakes in the two hands.
48. The god Vaidyanātha is seated in padmāsana (lotus seat). He is the lord Hara endowed with vanamālā etc. and with the lusture of a billion suns.
49. Endowed with a large armlet, adorned with a halo, Vaidyanātha should be understood as the bestower of power and freedom from diseases.
50. Bestowing boons, peaceful, carrying water-pot and rosary in the hands is Dhanvantri or Suṣeṇa or Vānarāṣya (Monkey-face) with divine limbs.
51. He has in his hands nectar and scripture (āgama). He should be drawn on both sides. He is the incarnation of the essences of Kāma and Lakṣmaṇa.

180 | THE UDAYESVARA TEMPLE

<!-- page 179 src=20260817_164122.jpg -->

# APARĀJITAPṚCCHĀ OF BHUVANADEVA
Chapter – 213

Viśvakarmā said –

1. With citron and the king of serpents, hour-glass shaped drum and spear, rich by the beauty of the tiara and adorned with all ornaments is Nandi.
2. With skull-topped mace, skull, hour-glass shaped drum and citron, the face fierce because of fangs is Mahākāla on the right.
Thus are the two pratihāras on the east.
3. With forefinger (extended), trident, as also mace and hour-glass shaped drum should Heramba be recollected on the left side and Bhrṅgi on the right.
4. With mace, hour-glass shaped drum, skull-topped mace and forefinger (extended) in the left hands both should be on the southern door and Bhrṅgi is auspicious on the right.
5. With a trident, hour-glass shaped drum, skull-topped mace, as also citron is Durmukha on the west (door) on the left side with a face which has the form of a cow.
6. On the western door, on the right, should be made Pāṇḍura with skull-topped mace, skull, hour-glass shaped drum and citron.
Thus Durmukha on the left and Pāṇḍura on the right.
7. With citron, lotus stalk, skull-topped mace and lotus stalk should Sita be arranged on the northern door on the left side.
8. With a lotus (part?), skull-topped mace, lotus stalk, and citron should be Asita on the northern door on the right side.
Thus Sita on the left and Asita on the right.
9. With boons-giving hands, with hands bearing the thunderbolt, goad and water-pot, sitting on an elephant, the thousand-eyed Indra stands to the east.
10. With boons-giving hands, (holding) a spear, lotus-stalk and water-pot, similar to a heap of flames, seated on a ram is Lord Agni.
11. Bearing a pen, book, cock and rod, seated on a huge buffalo, Yama is said to be dark-limbed.
12. With sword, shield, scissors and the head of the enemy in the hands, with long fangs protruding from the face, Nirṛti should be made seated on a corpse.*
13. With hands as boons-giving, holding the noose, lotus and water-pot, Varuna should be made riding a crocodile and in the west.
14. With hands in boons-giving (gesture) with flag, bearing a banner and water-pot, seated on a deer, green in colour is the Wind (God) guardian figure.
15. Bearing in his hands the mace, treasure, citron and water-pot, seated on an elephant, should be made Dhanada who should be in the north.
16. With boon-giving gesture, (bearing) a trident, snake and citron, seated on a bull should be made Īśāna of white hue.
17-20. Single faced, ten armed, dancing is Tripurāntaka. He wears a lion skin, an upper garment of deer skin and a red garment. The lord has the radiance of a billion suns and is ornamented with a garland of skulls and a head ornament of the moon. He bears a skull-topped mace, shield, sword, skull, kaṇṭhā (probably ghaṇṭā

* Chavānā (savānā) rīdhām should be śavāriḍham. It is surprising that the description should be in feminine gender but it possibly refers to the ākṛti or form.

APPENDIX I 181

<!-- page 180 src=20260817_164131.jpg -->

or bell) trident, bearing bow and arrow, noose and goad and wears two earrings (i.e. in both the ears). Hara should be made dancing in a circular form.

Thus the Tripurāntaka image along with the *dikpālas* or guardians of directions. Andhanārīśvara, having half the body of Umā. On the left side

21. Now I describe Andhanārīśvara, having half the body of Umā. On the left side

should be a breast and in the ear a palm leaf.

22. In the left ear should be an earring (*bālikā*\*) and in the right a *kuṇḍala* (earring).

Half of the diadem has ruby but on the right side is a pile of matted hair.

23. Half is the form of a woman adorned with all the ornaments. The left side is a

male with a skull and a girdle on the waist.

24. The right hand has a trident and rosary. In the left is a water pot and mirror as also Gaṇeśa.

This is Ardhānārīśvara.

25. Now I will describe the Umā-Maheśvara form which is Śaṅkara and Umā. In the right hand, he has the citron and trident.

26. One left hand is embracing (Umā) the other has a serpent. The hand of Umā is on the shoulder of Hara and in the other is a mirror.

27. Below should be shown the bull as also Kumāra and Gaṇeśa. Bhṛṅgī should also be made dancing, and without flesh (skinny).

This is the Umā-Maheśvara image.

28. Now I will describe Kṛṣṇa Śaṅkara. One half of him is Kṛṣṇa. On the Kṛṣṇa half should placed a diadem and on the right should be the matted locks.

29. In the right ear should be an earring, in the left a crocodile-shaped earring. There is also a rosary, trident, discus and conch.

This is the image of Kṛṣṇa Śaṅkara.

30-31. Seated on one pedestal, residing in one body, with six arms and four faces and endowed with all the characteristics, with a rosary, trident and mace in the right, water pot, skull-topped mace and discus in the left, such is the image of Hariharapitāmaha.

32-33. Four-faced, eight-armed, placed in one *cauki* or pedestal, erect face (non-tilted); the Sun is with lotus in hand, Rudra is auspicious to the right with skull-topped mace and trident, Pitāmaha should be made with water pot and rosary.

34. On the left is seated Hari with conch and discus. Thus should be made the image fulfilling all desires.

This is the image of Hariharahiranyagarbha.

35-36. With six arms and four faces, adorned with all the ornaments, having a water-pot and rosary in both the hands, the other hands (not clear), in the upper hands should be a lotus stalk of auspicious sign, endowed with all the ornaments and fulfilling all desires, such is the image of Candrārkapitāmaha.

* *Vālikā* has been emended to *bālikā*.

182

THE UDAVĀSVARA TEMPLE

<!-- page 181 src=20260817_164134.jpg -->

# APARĀJITAPṚCCHĀ OF BHUVANADEVA
Chapter - 214

Viśvakarmā said -

1-2. According to the differentiation of the Vedas like Rgveda etc. and ages like Kṛta and four armed Brahmā, Pitāmaha, Virañcī and Kamalāsana, so is four-faced them an image is prescribed of the Parmeṣṭhī (seated in the highest heaven).

3. In the right hand is the rosary, the upper (hand) has the śrūcā*. In the left hand is the book and below that the water-pot.

4. His image on the lotus seat is beneficent for all the varṇas or social classes. In the Kali Age, it will be so among all the excellent performers (i.e. perhaps those who perform the sacrifices).
This is Kamalāsana.

5. Rosary, book, śrūcā (śūcī) emended to śrūcā), water-pot will belong to the image of Virañcī and will give happiness in Dvāpara (Age).

6. A water-pot, rosary, ladle and book, these constitute the image of Pitāmaha which gives happiness in the Tretā.
This is Pitāmaha.

7. Book, rosary, ladle and water-pot, these belong to the image of Brahmā which gives happiness in the Kṛta Age.

8-9. Brahmā has a beautiful face, with an earring in the ear, with a diadem and garland which enhance his beauty. He has symmetrical shoulders, hair on the throat (beard), the lusture of molten gold and is bright with the necklaces of gems and jewels. Thus is the image of Brahmā.

10. I will speak of the image of Āditya (sun) along with the nine planets. He is of red colour, of great lusture and in the midst of a hundred-petal lotus.

11. He is endowed with all attributes and adorned with ornaments. He has two arms, a single face and has white lotuses in the hands.

12. He is in the midst of a round, radiant image and with red clothes. This is the form of Āditya, who is the destroyer of all sins.

13. Soma (Moon) is white and Aṅgāraka (Mars) is red. Budha or Mercury is of yellow colour and Bṛhaspati or Jupiter is of the same form*.

14. Śukra or Venus is white like the milk of a cow, Śāni or Saturn is of black colour.
Rāhu is of blue colour and Ketu is always to be grey.

15. Soma or Moon should have a lotus in hand, Mars should have a rod and water-pot. Mercury should be shown in yogāsana and Jupiter with rosary and water-pot.

16. Venus should have rosary and water-pot, Saturn with rod and water-pot. Rāhu should be with half a body, Ketu should have the form of a cupped hand (?)*.

17. Āditya should be on a chariot of seven horses and the Moon with ten horses.
Mars should be on a ram and Mercury should be on a snake-seat.

* Śūcī is obviously a mistake for śrūcā. Sthitā qualifies a feminine noun. Śūcī cannot be such a noun. Śrūcā is feminine, it gives the right meaning and fits in the metre which is anaṣṭuṇa.

+ That is, of similar colour. Jupiter, too, is of yellow colour. It is surprising that Mercury, which is generally associated with green colour traditionally, should here be associated with yellow.

▫ Does this indicate a curved body?

APPENDIX I 183

<!-- page 182 src=20260817_164146.jpg -->

18. Jupiter should be known as mounted on a swan. Venus should have a frog mount and Saturn should be on a buffalo. Rāhu is in the middle of a tank.
19. Ketu should have the form of a snake's tail, Saturn has fierce fangs. (They) should be with tiara, necklace and splendidly adorned with ornaments.
Thus is the image of Sun with the planets.

184

THE UDAYESVARA TEMPLE

<!-- page 183 src=20260817_164149.jpg -->

# APARĀJITAPṚCCHĀ OF BHUVANADEVA
Chapter – 219

1. Now I will speak of the characteristics of the jalaśāyi (Viṣṇu lying on the cosmic waters). He is lying on the couch constituted by (the great serpent) Śeṣa*.

2. Eight tālas in length.* The total circumference is nine tālas, seated in ease in the form of a liṅga°.

3. The circular (couch) would be like the form of the eternal matrix†. One hand (of jalaśāyi Viṣṇu) is under the head (in a reclining position) and the other is placed on the lotus of the heart.

4. In the left hand is the mace and on the upper is the sudarśana cakra (Viṣṇu's discus). He is adorned with a diadem, necklace and floral garland.

5. He wears a necklace and armlets and earrings (in both ears). On the edge of the diadem the seven hoods of the serpent are like a garland.

6. These are seven pots bearing nectar and below are the nine nāga-kulas⁶. Lakṣmī is close to the feet⁷ and Garuḍa has palms folded in veneration (añjali mudrā).

7. Brahma, who is designated as 'born of the lotus' is at the navel lotus. The seven worlds should be depicted in attendance.

8. The ten incarnations should be shown beginning with the churning of the ocean by the Mandāra mountain (i.e. Kūrma or tortoise incarnation). Thus should be shown Viṣṇu on the cosmic waters.

9. In assistance should be shown Keśava, Trivikram, Dāmodara as well as Adhokṣaja. No other icon is regarded as praiseworthy.

Thus Viṣṇu on the cosmic waters.

10. The height should be of seven parts and the length of nine parts⁸, the head is to be of two parts and the snout of two parts.

11. Two and half parts is the neck⁹. There are the three (auspicious) folds on the neck totaling to two and a half.

12. The head should be half the part of the shoulderᶜ and the legs of four parts. So should be shown the back, the form resembling that of the elephant.

* The latter half of the second line is not clear since 'ne' appears to be a mistake and there is one syllable missing. The editor, too, has put a question mark there.

+ Latter half is again not clear.
° The verse is certainly quite cryptic, but a careful study conveys the essence. It seems to describe Śeṣanāga, coiled with his hood upright serving as a canopy. On this serpent couch reclines Viṣṇu. It seems that nine tālas is the diameter of the coiled serpent. If we take it to mean the circumference then the diameter would be less than three tālas and at most three, if the ᾧ is approximated to three as a rough rule of thumb. Eight tālas seems to refer to the height of the hood projecting straight upwards like a liṅga.

† Viṣṇu lying on the serpent couch in the ocean is symbolic of the divine power in the matrix of time before creation. The oceanic waters symbolize the unmanifest (aryukta) state of nature before creation, and the coiled serpent is the symbol of time.

§ There are possibly nine figures (partially anthropomorphic?) of serpent species surrounding the god. Possibly seven of them bear pots of nectar from the ocean.

• Lakṣmī is possibly in the act of massaging Viṣṇu's feet.
§ Since it is the height and length mentioned, the form of the Varāha is theriomorphic.

° First half of the line is not clear.

ᶜ Mastakārdhubhāgaḥ seems to be a bahuorihi (mastakaḥ ardhubhāgaḥ yasya), otherwise the shoulders would be too small.

<!-- page 184 src=20260817_164156.jpg -->

13. The front feet are two and a half parts, the other two feet are of two portions. The protuberance is of three portions and one part for the *akṣadesa*.\*

14. The front part of the feet are half a part and the hooves are a quarter part.† The externals are to be made of more than two parts. One should start this sequence from one and a half parts.

15. The knee should be half a part, the palate (?) is one part. The calf of the legs are the same as the arms.

16. The two abdomens (?)□ should be four parts. It should be well rounded. The tip of the snout (*puṣkara*) should be one-fourth.†

17. The tip of the snout should be properly expanded,§ rounded and well positioned. It should have two holes and the face should have two tusks.

18. The forehead should be half of three times of the *puṣkara*. One-fourth should be the ear which should be erect from the front.

19. If measured with a string from one ear to another, then half of that would be the eyes. The eyes should be like that of an elephant as (also) the forehead which should be like that of an elephant.

20. The left foreleg is raised and the other is in sequence.\*

21. One-fourth of the forehead will be the measurement of the sign indicating the spine. Circumambulating it, the creatures of the three worlds should be shown.

22. In this way, the tail and the knot of one throat should be made in front of the heart.§ In the lower half a tortoise should be made and in the half of that a water body.£

23. Thus should be made the proportions of the Varāha which have been said. At the tip of the tusk, on the left side, should be Lakṣmī and the right hand should be at the tusk.

24. I will describe separately one by one the images of Vaikuṇṭha, Viśvarūpa, Ananta and Trailokyamohana.

25. Now I describe Vaikuṇṭha. He is with eight arms and of great prowess. He is to be made seated on Garuḍa and of four faces by those desirous of peace.

26. The four weapons on the right are mace, sword, arrow and discus. The four weapons on the left are conch-shell, shield, bow and lotus.

27. The front face of Viṣṇu should be that of a man, on the south should be that of Narasimha, on the west the form should be that of the face of Śrī, on the north face should be that (form) of Varāha.

\*aksa is axis. Meaning is not clear here.

+ This is if emendation is made to the text as bhāgapādaḥ khurah mataḥ.

This means possibly both the upper abdomen and the lower abdomen.

† The Sanskrit lexicons have 'puṣkaram kariḥastāgre'; puṣkara is the tip of the tusk (hand of the elephant). In case of the Varāha, this may be understood as the tip of the snout. The last part of the verse is not clear.

§ Not too long, not too short.

The first line is not clear.

It seems that the painting of the Varāha is referred to here where the spine can only be indicated and not explicitly shown. This is strengthened by the second line of this verse.

The meaning does not construe.

The tortoise is also an incarnation of Viṣṇu. It seems that the making of this entire composition is being discussed here.

186

THE UDAYESVARA TEMPLE

<!-- page 185 src=20260817_164159.jpg -->

Thus Vaikuṇṭha.

28. Viśvarūpa, is of great radiance, omniscient and cause of creation. I will now speak of his twenty arms, one by one.

29. In his right hands are banner, plough, conch, thunderbolt, goad, as well as arrow, discus, citron and boons-giving hand.

30. In his left hands are banner, rod, noose, mace, bow, lotus, horn, club and rosary.

31. The (remaining) two hands are in the stance of yoga (yogamudrā) and he is seated on Garuḍa. Man, Naraśimha, Śrīṃukha and Boar-faced (these are his four faces).

32. He is the creator of the universe, the cosmic image of the world, the source of great origin. He should be made endowed with twenty Śaktis, he is the bestower of all desires.

Thus Viśvarūpa.

33. Ananta is the source of infinite forms as he is the source of infinite creation. He is enveloped by infinite powers and is the source of infinite powers.

34. He is endowed with twelve arms, four faces and is of great celebration. He is mentioned as having an eagle banner and should be made as such. He is the bestower of all boons.

35. Mace, sword discus as well as thunderbolt, goad and boons-giving, thus he has six excellent weapons in his right hand.

36. Conch, shield, bow, lotus, rod as well as noose, these are said to be the weapons in his left hand.

37. The faces should be of man, Naraśimha, Śrī and boar, full of a mass of effulgence should be made Ananta.

Thus Ananta.

38. I will now speak of Trailokyamohana who is the cause of illusion or fascination in the universe. He is endowed with sixteen arms, is seated on Garuḍa and is of great prowess.

39. Mace, thunderbolt, goad, arrow, spear, discus as well as protective gesture, these are in order the weapons in his right hands.

40. A club, noose, bow, conch, lotus, water-pot, horn are in his left hands and two hands are in the stance of yoga (yogamudrā).

41. He has the faces of man, Naraśimha, boar and Kapila. Trailokyamohana should be made with sixteen Śaktis.

Thus Trailokyamohana.

42-43. Now I will speak of Garuḍa who in his magnitude/proportion is like his master. He is to be made in front of Viṣṇu and not any other God. The measure of the image at the base (further reading not clear). The image for worship at the base in terms of its length is of nine tālas.

44. Five, six, seven and two aṅgulas (not clear). So much of the male organ, navel and chest of the vāhana, i.e. Garuḍa should be in front.

45. The two feet, knees and the waist of the image of the vehicle (vāhana), i.e. Garuḍa, should be as far as the sight. The frontal stance of the claws should be made ferocious.

46. The form should be like that of a man. His nose should be high, sharp and aggressive. Over the arms are the two wings and there is the ornamentation of the diadem.

APPENDIX I 187

<!-- page 186 src=20260817_164210.jpg -->

47. The left foot has the knee on the ground, the other foot has a knee flexed (behind). The fore-part of the lotus-like foot is well modelled and the image is made beautiful by the height of the wings.

48. In front of Śiva should be the buffalo, in front of Viṣṇu the king of birds, in front of Brahmā should always be a white swan.

49. In front of Sūrya is (shown) the sky and in front of Devī is a pedestal*. In front of the lotus-like foot of the image there is a pedestal of equal height.

Thus is Garuḍa.

50. Now I shall describe the *pratihāras* (door keepers) who are eight on four sides. I will mention in order their features as they are.

51. The superior ones are all to be made of dwarfish size. The first should have the forefinger (extended) conch-shell, discus and rod.

52. On the left and right are (the *pratihāras*) named Caṇḍa and Pracaṇḍa. On the left is Caṇḍa and Pracaṇḍa should be on the right.

53. Lotus, sword, shield, mace, should be given from (the direction of) left to right. Jaya and Vijaya have lotus and mace in the reverse order.

54. Forefinger, arrow, bow and mace is from left to right. Dhātā and the mace are to the right. Vidhātā is also similar.

55. Forefinger, lotus, conch, mace is from left to right. Bhadra is to the right of the weapons and so is Subhadra.

* *Pāduḷā* cannot mean slippers which would be absurd. The word may be *piṭhikā* or pedestal.

# APARĀJITAPRCCHĀ OF BHUVANADEVA
Chapter - 220

1. I will speak of the eight pratihāras of Brahma one by one. They are anthropomorphic in form, solemn, with beard and bright diadems. 2. Satya should be on the left with lotus, ladle, book and rod, Dharmaka should be on the right with weapons on the right shoulder.
3. Priyodbhava should be on the left with rosary, lotus, book and rod. Yajña, the bestower of all boons, should be with rod, book, ladle and writing board. 4. The one named Vijaya should be with rosary, mace, shield and rod. Yajñabhadraka should have the right hand downwards holding a fruit.
5. Bhava, the bestower of all boons, should be with rosary, noose, goad and rod. Vibhava, the bestower of all peace, should be with rod, goad, noose and lotus. Thus the eight Pratihāras of Brahma.
6-7. Dandī and Pingala, Ānanda as well as Nandaka, Citra, Vicitra, Kiranākṣa and Sulocana - all these should be made of anthropomorphic form by these desirous of peace. There eight should be on the four doors their order from left to right.
8. Tarjanī (extended forefinger), ray (kirana?), cock and rod should be of Dandī on the left, so also listen to that of Pingala.
9. Spear in place of kirana, ray (anisakah) in place of cock, extended forefinger and rod as (mentioned) before, thus is Pingala on the south-east.
10. Of Anandaka should be remembered the two extended forefingers, thunderbolt and rod. On the right, Dantaka should be with extended forefinger and rod.
11. On the left, Citraka should be remembered with two extended forefingers, lotus and rod. On the right, Vicitra should stand with extended forefinger and rod.
12. Kiranākṣa is with two extended forefingers, ray, and rod. The pratihāra Sulocana is with extended forefinger and rod on the right.
13. These are the eight pratihāras who destroy all obstacles. They are all effulgent and each one of them belongs to Sūrya. Thus are the eight pratihāras of Bhāskara.
14-15. Avighna and Vighnarāja, Suvaktra, also Balavāna, Gajakarna, Gokarna, Saumya and Bhayadāyaka - all of these are of dwarf forms, giving happiness and with faces of men. In the hands of Avighna are extended forefinger, axe, lotus and rod.
16. Extended forefinger and rod on the right, that should be Vighnarāja. On both sides of the east door is the destroyer of all obstacles.
17. Extended forefinger, sword, shield and rod are in the hands of Suvakatraka. Extended forefinger and rod on the right are mentioned for Balavāna. the west stands Gokarna with extended forefinger, bow and arrow rod, discus and lotus. 18. Karṇaka is with extended forefinger, bow and arrow rod, discus and lotus. the west stands Gokarna with extended forefinger and rod on the right. the west stands Gokarna with extended forefinger, lotus, goad and rod. Abhayadāyaka is to be with weapons on the right shoulder.
20. These destroyer of obstacles (vighnavināsanāh) are to be placed on the two sides of the doors in all directions. Thus these eight pratihāras by those desirous of peace of kings. Thus are the Gaṇeśa pratihāras.
21. Jayā, Vijayā as also Ajitā and Aparājitā, Nibhaktā, Maṅgalā, Mohinī as well as Stambhinī.

APPENDIX I 189

<!-- page 188 src=20260817_164220.jpg -->

22. Jayā is towards right with protective gesture, goad, noose and rod. Vijayā's

weapons are mentioned from left to right.

23. Gesture of protection*, lotus, noose and rod are (attributes) for Ajitā. On the left is Aparājitā. Gesture of protection, thunderbolt, goad and rod are (attributes) for Nibhaktā, on the right is Mangalā.

24. Gesture of protection, conch, lotus and rod are for the one named Mohinī. If the weapons on the right are added then it is Stambhinī.

25. In the temple of Gaurī are desirable these door-keepers.
Thus the door-keepers of Gaurī.
In the same way I will speak in order of Caṇḍikā (Caṇḍikā's door keepers).

26. Vetāla and also Koṭara, Piṅgākṣa as well as Bhṛkuṭi, Dhūmraka as also Kaṅkaṭa, Raktākṣa and Sulocana (are the door-keepers).

27. The face is fierce because of the teeth and the teeth flash in anger. These are barbarians who are dark-limbed, with red-shot eyes*, and of great strength.

28. Extended forefinger, as also skull-topped mace, hour-glass shaped drum and rod are the weapons of one who is called Vaitāla. On the right is Koṭara.

29. Piṅgākṣa has protective gesture, sword, shield and rod. By adding protective gesture in the right position should be the one named Bhṛkuṭi.

30. The one named Dhūmraka has extended forefinger, thunderbolt, goad and rod. By adding the right and left positions should be the one named Kaṅkaṭa.

31. To the left is the one who is Raktākṣa by name and has an extended forefinger, trident, skull-topped mace as also rod. On the right is Trilocana.°

32. On the two sides of each of the doors on the four quarters stand the Destroyers of Obstacles (vighnavināśanāḥ). These eight are the pratihāras or door-keepers of Devīs agreed to by all.
Thus the eight pratihāras of Caṇḍikā.

33. Indra and also Indrajaya, Mahendra, Vijayendraka, Dharanedra, Padmaka, Sunābha, Suradundubhi (are the door-keepers).

34. These eight are the pratihāras with peaceful colours like yellow etc. I will speak of them separately one by one in order.

35. Indra has fruit, thunderbolt, goad and rod and in the right is Indrajaya. Mahendra has two thunderbolts, fruit and rod and on the left is Vijaya?

36. Arising from these weapons are three/five snake-hoods.† Dharanendra, Padmaka and all are to be remembered as peaceful.

37-38. These have been forms and functions of yakṣas, carry treasure in their hands, have well-modelled abdomens. All of them are, without doubt, givers of peace. Sunābha, Suradundubhi, these eight are the pratihāras of Jinendra and are bestowers of peace. In front of the town and village they are the destroyers of all obstacles.

* Abhi in the text should be abhaya.

+ Maraktākṣā is possibly āraktākṣā.

° Possibly another named for Sulocana.

† Text seems corrupt and meaning is not clear. Perhaps it indicates snake-hoods above Dharanendra and Padmaka.

THE UDAYESVARA TEMPLE

<!-- page 189 src=20260817_164225.jpg -->

# APARĀJITAPṚCCHĀ OF BHUVANADEVA
Chapter – 222

1. I will now declare the proportions of Gaurī constituting the characteristics of her icon. The whole dependent on the brahmasūtra (plumb line) should be of one hundred aṅgulas.
2. The face is of thirteen aṅgulas, the neck is of three aṅgulas. The (measurement of the) rounded breasts is eighteen aṅgulas.*
3. The navel is twelve aṅgulas and the stomach four.* She is endowed with the three folds on the abdomen and the private part is of seven aṅgulas.
4. The thigh is of twenty-two aṅgulas. The knees are of three aṅgulas. The nalaka (shank) should be of the measure of the thigh and the foot (i.e. from the sole to the ankle) should have the same measure as the knees.
5. With four arms, three eyes and adorned by all ornaments, she is to be made seated on an iguana and is the bestower of all desires.
6. (The names of the twelve Gaurīs are) Umā and Pārvatī, Gaurī, Lalitā and the excellent Śriyā, Hemavatī, Rambhā and also Sāvitrī.
7. Trikhaṇḍā°, Totalā as also Tripurā who is held to be the twelfth (Gaurī). Thus the twelve images and Śivaśāsanī¹.
8. With rosary, lotus, mirror and water-pot, such an image of Umā is worshipped by the gods also.
9. Pārvatī, born of the (Himālaya) mountain has rosary, waterpot, Śiva (linga) and the chief of the gaṇas (Gaṇeśa) and has on both the sides the agni kuṇḍa or pits of fire.
10. The image of Gaurī has rosary, protective hand gesture, lotus and the water-pot below. So also should be made that of Śivaśāsanī.
11. Lalitā is the name (of the goddess) who has attributes of spear, rosary, the lute and the water-pot and who is served by celestial beings, viz. siddhas (adepts) and cāraṇas (bards).
12. The icon of (the goddess named) Śrī is with rosary, lotus and with protection and boons-giving gestures. She has an iguana seat. Śrī should always be worshipped in the home.
13. With rosary, water-pot and palms joined in añjali mudrā° at the heart, (standing) in the midst of five agni kuṇḍas is the splendid Kṛṣṇā.
14. Himavān is the mountain king and his offspring is Girīndrajā. On being married she is (called) Maheśvarī and is with a lotus and mirror.
15. With water-pot, rosary, thunderbolt and goad and on an elephant, thus of incomparable beauty is Rambhā, the bestower of all boons.
16. With rosary, book and lotus and a water-pot below, the four faced Sāvitrī is placed in the house of brahmins.
17. With rosary, thunderbolt, spear and a water-pot below, is Trikhaṇḍā° who is the bestower of all desires is to be venerated for prosperity.

* This perhaps indicates the diameter; the latter half of the line is not clear.

+ The text seems to be corrupt. How can the navel be bigger than abdomen?
° The editor has emended Triṣaṇḍā to Śrikhaṇḍā. Actually, it should be Trikhaṇḍā which is another name for Lalitā.

† Line not clear.
§ Sampurāñcaliḥ does not make sense and hence has been emended to sampuṭāñjuliḥ.
• Refer to footnote 3.

APPENDIX I 191

<!-- page 190 src=20260817_164236.jpg -->

18. With rosary, rod, shield as well as fly-whisk, of white colour and destroyer of poison, the Goddess Totalā is worshipped by meditation.

19. With noose, goad and protection and boons-giving gestures, the four-armed Tripurā is worthy of being worshipped and is extolled by the gods too.

20. I shall now speak of the five Lalīs differentiated on the basis of their weapons; Līlayā, Līlā, Līlāṅgī, Lalitā and Līlāvatī.

21. They are spoken of in order of their seats. They are four-armed, with complexion glowing like that of molten gold and lusture similar to that of the rising sun.

22. They are with beautiful faces, beautiful eyes, beautiful forms, the bestowers of beauty, with red lips, conch like necks, eyes elongated up to their karṇapūras (earrings).

23. The lower hands should be in the boons-giving gesture with rosary and water-pot. Thus, the two hands have been spoken of as similar.

23-24. In the upper two hands Lalīyā has a pair of lotus. Līlā has a lotus and book, Līlāṅgī has noose and lotus and Lalitā has thunderbolt and goad.

25. Līlāvatī has noose and goad. Thus are the five Lalīyās spoken of. After this, the nine types of Durgās will be spoken of by me.

26-27. In the beginning is Mahālakṣmī, Nandā as well as Kṣemakarī, Śivadūtī, Mahāraṇḍā, Bhramarī, Sarvamaṅgalā, Revatī and Harasiddhi; (thus) are spoken of the nine Durgās. O Aparājitā! Now I speak of each one separately in order.

28. Mahālakṣmī, the bestower of happiness, bears the boons-giving gesture, trident, shield and drinking cup and has a snake in the blue-neck.

29. (With) rosary, sword, shield as well as drinking-cup, Nandā is well known and is venerated for the purpose of giving pleasure.

30. With boons-giving gesture, trident, lotus and drinking cup in the hands, thus is the one named Kṣemakarī, the bestower of well-being and good-health.

31. With water-pot, discus and shield as well as an excellent drinking-cup, thus is Śivadutī, the bestower of all prosperity.

32. With sword, trident, bell as well as an excellent shield, Mahāraṇḍā is the name venerated by gods too.

33. Bearing a sword, hour-glass shaped drum, shield and drinking-cup, Bhramarī is the name of the one who misleads the wicked-hearted.

34. With rosary, thunderbolt, bell as well as and excellent drinking-cup, the auspicious Sarvamaṅgalā is the destroyer of all obstacles.

35. Bearing a rod, trident, skull-topped mace and a drinking-cup, Revatī is the name of the one who is the bestower of peace on all.

36. With a water-pot, sword, hour-glass shaped drum and a drinking-cup, Harasiddhi is known to be the cause of all success. Thus are the nine Durgās.

37. Gaurī should be seated on an iguana, Lalīyā has the swan as a vehicle. Durgā should be seated on a lion and (these) are the bestower of all desires.

THE UDAYESVARA TEMPLE

# APARĀJITAPṚCCHĀ OF BHUVANADEVA
Chapter – 223

Vīśvakarmā said:

1. Listen to the attributes of Cāmunḍā, the goddess who was the slayer of Caṇḍa. She is emaciated, of cruel form, with a formidable nose and sunken belly. She is tawny, her hair standing erect, dark-limbed, with red eyes, with flashing eyes and teeth and terrible nails and face.
2. She has a trident, sword and shield, bow and arrow, noose, goad, bell, mirror and thunderbolt. She has the rod, axe, conch-shell, discus, mace and club. She is to be with lifted corners of the mouth (i.e. smiling a ghostly sort of a smile) and bearing a skull garland. Cāmunḍā is seated on a corpse and has bells hanging down the shanks.
3. She wears a garment of tiger skin, ornaments of serpents, anklets with deep sound and is another (form of) death to the demons. Thus is the image of Cāmunḍā.
4. Now I will speak of Kātyāyanī, whose command arises from the rulers. She bestows glory and valour and arouses the happiness of kings.
5. In the right hands are the spear, sword, thunderbolt, discus and arrow. In the left are the bow, shield, noose, bell and goad.
8-9. Mahiṣa should be shown below with decapitated head at the feet. Know that the right (foot) should be on Mahiṣa. The left foot should be shown on a lotus pedestal. That demon should be shown with decapitated head and red-coloured, i.e. blood-stained clothes.
10. The goddess having seized the forelocks by the left hand, pierces the heart by the spear, and the beautiful (foot?) is on the belly of the demon.
11. The lion, who is the mount of the goddess, pounces on top of Mahiṣa, the demon. The goddess by the name of Kātyāyani should be made thus. Thus Kātyāyani
12. After the mother goddesses, I will speak of Bhairava, gaṇas etc. O son! Vīreśa should be made with a vīṇā in hand and dancing.
13. Thereafter, Gaṇanātha should be made with an elephant face and colossal (form). In the beginning should be made Gaṇanātha and in the end, Bhairava.
14. Amidst the row of goddesses should be made Caṇḍikā etc. I will now speak of them according to sequence. Listen with an attentive mind.
15. With a string of skulls in her ears and with skull-topped mace and spear, emaciated, of cruel form, with bright teeth, is Cāmunḍā.
16. With rosary, sword, bell and water-pot, seated on a buffalo, with the face of a boar, is Vārāhī, who fulfills all wishes.
17. Seated on an elephant and (having) rosary, rod, noose and water-pot, should be made (the form of) goddess Indrāṇī. Cāmunḍā should be made on a preta or ghost.
18. (With) rosary, spear, cock and water-pot, seated on a peacock, Kaumārī should be known as six-faced.
19. (With) rosary, ladle, book as also water-pot, four-faced is Brahmāṇī, seated on a swan and the fulfiller of all desires.
20. With mace, discus, conch-shell and lotus, adorned with earrings and seated on Garuḍa, Vaiṣṇavī fulfills all wishes.

APPENDIX | 193

21. Seated on a bull, with khaṅga (type of weapon), spear, sword, skull-topped mace and citron, with matted locks and the diadem adorned with the (crescent) moon, white limbed, is Maheśvarī.
Thus the images of the mother goddesses.
